# Red Sea Global sells stays through parallel channels

Evidence-based due diligence on Red Sea Global (RSG), its brands, destinations, public digital ecosystem and resort booking journeys, with implications for a possible AI agent proof of concept. All evidence was gathered on 12 October 2026. This document is research. It does not design or build anything, and the agent concept it discusses is a proposal under evaluation, not an existing RSG capability.

How to read citations: a tag such as [R001] is a Source ID. Every Source ID resolves to a URL in Section 19 and in the link definitions at the end of the file. A citation to a booking-screen URL is a citation to a recorded interface observation made at that URL on 12 October 2026.

## 1. Executive summary

The evidence gathered on 12 October 2026 establishes that booking a Red Sea Global resort is not one journey. It is at least five separate ones, and none of them could be observed to completion under the research rules. A guest can book on RSG's own destination sites (visitredsea.com and amaala.com), where the published booking terms name Regenerative Escapes Tourism Company (RETC) as the contracting party [R168]. A guest can book RSG-operated resorts on a second, separate engine branded Red Sea Global Hospitality [R227]. A guest can book third-party resorts directly on eight operator engines that were traced, plus Hyatt, which blocked access [R239] [R242]. One resort (Jayasom) is handed off from amaala.com to its operator [R248], and Thuwal Private Retreat is enquiry only [R127]. These channels do not share sessions, identifiers or rate names. For the same RSG-operated resorts and the same dates, RSG's two own channels returned different availability [R220] [R228], presented tax differently [R219] [R230] and published different cancellation windows [R158] [R122].

Three further findings shape any discussion of an AI agent proof of concept. First, every route stopped at the guest-details page. Payment, verification, submission and confirmation are known only from published statements, and one form label indicates an email verification code [R223] [R168] [R144]. Second, the public terms do not grant access. The booking terms limit use to "personal and non-commercial purposes" and prohibit extracting "information relating to hotel pricing and availability", including by "'screen scraping' software" [R168]. No RSG developer documentation, partner programme or sandbox was found [R292], and site terms reserve all content, logo and trademark rights [R003]. Third, official content is not a reliable knowledge source as it stands. Section 15 registers 128 conflicts, most of them between two official sources, covering hotel counts, opening status, policies, airports, taxes and names.

On the company itself, the evidence is consistent that RSG is a closed joint stock company owned by the Public Investment Fund [R004] [R036], that it reports "19 resorts open across its destinations: 11 at The Red Sea, seven at AMAALA, and Thuwal Private Retreat" as of 1 October 2026 [R083], and that it presents four brand layers with separately implemented visual systems and no published brand guidelines [R023]. No official RSG source announces a guest-facing AI assistant. One vendor blog, read through a summarising tool and uncorroborated, describes an "Agentic Tourism Corridor" involving RSG [R303].

What this implies for a possible proof of concept is limited and specific. The evidence supports business discussion, question preparation and content scoping. It does not support any statement that a capability is feasible or ready. Each of the thirteen capabilities assessed in Section 13 depends on something that only RSG, RETC, RSG Hospitality or an operator can supply: written permission, an authoritative data source, a test environment, brand rules, or answers to the questions in Sections 12 and 18. The research itself carries disclosures that the reader should weigh, set out in Section 2: one researcher replayed the destination site's search request about 35 times, a Marriott rate click started a timed hold, an SLS rate click created a draft booking identifier, no screenshots exist, and the mobile app and Hyatt properties were not examined.

## 2. Research scope, dates, tools and limitations

### 2.1 Scope and question

The research question was what evidence gathered on 12 October 2026 establishes about RSG, its brand architecture, its destinations and resorts, its public digital ecosystem and the complete customer journey for discovering, selecting, registering for and booking a resort stay, and what that evidence implies for a possible AI agent proof of concept. This report synthesises nine research notes files. It adds no fact from outside those notes. Where the notes do not establish something, the report says "Not verified" and names the evidence or action required.

### 2.2 Dates and workstreams

Every source was accessed on 12 October 2026. Live observations were made between about 18:45 and 19:23 UTC (21:45 to 22:23 in Saudi Arabia).

| Notes file | Workstream | Main method | Main limitation |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | Company profile | RSG, PIF and Vision 2030 pages retrieved by direct HTTP request and converted to text. About 50 of 407 media centre items opened | Text behind scripts, toggles, images and PDF viewers was not captured. Two third-party articles were read through a summarising fetch tool |
| 02 | Brand architecture | Live HTML, CSS and rendered pages of 13 official sites | Corporate page text captured in truncated form. No official brand specification exists to compare against |
| 03 | Digital ecosystem, UX and accounts | Built-in browser at 1024x768 and 375x812 | Nothing submitted. Chat not opened. Shared browser profile |
| 04 | Inventory, The Red Sea | Destination pages, RSG releases, operator pages | No availability search. Hyatt blocked. Two sources read through a summarising fetch tool |
| 05 | Inventory, AMAALA and other | amaala.com in the browser, RSG releases, operator pages | No availability search. Three operator pages read through a summarising fetch tool |
| 06 | Booking on RSG-owned channels | Built-in browser walk of two engines, plus replay of the site's own search request | Stopped at guest details. No UI search on amaala.com |
| 07 | Booking on operator engines | Built-in browser walk of eight engines | Stopped at guest details. Hyatt blocked. Mostly entered by deep link |
| 08 | Arrival, travel requirements and support | RSG sites, RSI airport site, Saudi government sites | Booking flow not walked. UI text strings read from page source |
| 09 | Privacy, terms and technical footprint | Legal pages, page source, consent configuration, store listings | Short fragments only. Operator terms read by keyword or summary |

### 2.3 Evidence classes used throughout

| Class label in this report | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Officially documented** | Text on a page or document published by RSG, PIF, a Saudi government body or the operator of the service, opened and read on the access date |
| **Directly observed** (or "observed live") | Seen in the rendered live interface in a browser on the access date |
| **Fetched HTML** | Read from fetched markup or the page's DOM without being displayed. Not interactive evidence |
| **API output** | Obtained by replaying the destination site's own search request with dates and guest counts only. Not a rendered screen |
| **Summarised** | Read through a fetch tool that returns a summary and not raw text. Wording carries paraphrase risk |
| **Third-party** | A publisher other than the owner of the fact. Uncorroborated unless stated |
| **Indicative** | A vendor or platform inferred from file names or hostnames. Not confirmed by RSG |
| **Conflicting** | Two opened sources disagree. Both are shown and the conflict is not resolved here |
| **Inference in the notes** | A reading made by a researcher, kept marked as inference |
| **Not verified** | No opened source or observation supports it. The evidence or action required is named |

Status words are also kept apart: announced, planned, under development, open, and bookable are different facts. A policy statement always names the channel it came from. Form types (corporate inquiry, newsletter, vendor registration, residential sales inquiry, account creation, loyalty enrollment, booking inquiry, actual reservation, guest registration or check-in) are recorded separately in Sections 5.7 and 10.

### 2.4 Disclosures that affect how the evidence should be read

1. No reservation, payment, account or enquiry was made on any site, and no personal data was entered anywhere. No name, email, phone, card or identity data was typed. No terms box was ticked and no submit, confirm, pay or enquiry button was pressed [R223] [R239].
2. No screenshots were saved to disk. This was a tool limitation. All evidence is a textual observation log: page text, DOM reads and network reads, transcribed with the URL and time. Where a note describes a screenshot, the image itself does not exist.
3. The mobile app was not installed or tested. Only its store listings and the official app page were read [R152] [R153] [R105].
4. Hyatt properties (Grand Hyatt The Red Sea and Miraval The Red Sea on hyatt.com) were blocked by bot protection. The protection was not circumvented, so nothing about the Hyatt channel is known [R242] [R187].
5. One researcher replayed the visitredsea.com search request about 35 times across visitredsea.com and amaala.com to find dates with availability. The requests re-used the page's own request shape and sent only hotel ID, dates and guest counts [R225]. This should be read alongside the booking terms on the same site, under which a user agrees not to gather or extract material "specifically including information relating to hotel pricing and availability", whether or not by "spiders or other 'screen scraping' software" [R168]. This report does not judge whether the replay breached that clause. It recommends that the replay is not repeated without written permission.
6. Selecting a rate on Marriott's engine started a timed room hold ("Room(s) held for 14:54"), which was left to expire. Selecting a rate on the SLS engine created a draft booking identifier in the URL. No further rate was selected on any other Marriott property [R239] [R243].
7. Cookie banners were mostly not seen, because the shared browser profile already held a necessary-only consent state when most researchers arrived. That state was set on visitredsea.com through the consent preference centre at about 18:45 to 18:46 UTC by the booking workstreams [R095] [R102] [R095]. Banner wording was read from the consent tool's configuration, which was served for a visitor location reported as Egypt [R095]. On operator sites, banners were declined where a decline option existed and left unanswered where the only one-click option was accept [R240] [R241].
8. Legal text was quoted only in short fragments. Every legal statement in this report is a close paraphrase or a fragment and must be re-read at source before any legal use. None of the RSG-family legal documents shows a last-updated date [R291] [R168].

### 2.5 Other limitations

The research session appears to have been located in Egypt, which affected default currencies (Six Senses defaulted to EGP) and the consent rule set served [R241] [R095]. redseaglobal.com and amaala.com returned an HTTP 403 challenge page to non-browser tools and were read in a browser. No challenge was solved or bypassed [R138] [R138]. Several sources were read through a summarising fetch tool: the Rosewood AMAALA, Jayasom and Ritz-Carlton AMAALA operator pages [R213] [R216] [R218], the Four Seasons press release and SLS page text [R180] [R178], the App Store listing [R152], The New Arab and Arab News articles [R084] [R085], the Adobe release and Globant blog [R301] [R303], and the Accor and SynXis developer pages [R253] [R254]. The display currency on visitredsea.com was changed to USD for one test and had reverted to SAR by the end [R101]. A hidden browser pane made some clicks unreliable, which is why several operator engines were entered by deep link and not through their own search forms [R239]. Prices are single observations for one scenario at one moment and are not quotes.

## 3. Company profile, history and story

Evidence class for this section: officially documented (RSG, PIF and Vision 2030 pages retrieved by direct HTTP GET and converted to text), plus three third-party articles, two of which were read through a summarising fetch tool. Nothing in this section was verified on the ground or through a booking engine. A marketing page or press release establishes what RSG says, not what is true on site.

### 3.1 Name, legal form and addresses

The public name is "Red Sea Global" (RSG). The English footer reads "© 2026, Red Sea Global. All rights reserved." [R001]. The Arabic About page uses "البحر الأحمر الدولية" and the Arabic terms open with "شركة البحر الأحمر الدولية" [R005] [R006]. The former name is The Red Sea Development Company (TRSDC): the 25 October 2022 release says "The responsible developer, formerly known as The Red Sea Development Company" [R037].

The Privacy Policy on redseaglobal.com describes RSG as "a closed joint stock company owned by the Public Investment Fund of Saudi Arabia" and as the data controller for that site [R004]. The English terms say RSG "is registered in the Kingdom of Saudi Arabia" and are governed by "The Laws of the Kingdom of Saudi Arabia" [R003].

| Item | What the official page says | Source | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Registered office (English terms)** | "5th Floor, MU04 Tower, ITCC Complex, AlRaidah Digital City, Al Nakhil District 3807, Riyadh 12382 – 6726, Saudi Arabia" | [R003] | Officially documented |
| **Registered office (Arabic terms)** | Third floor, building IN01, ITCC complex | [R006] | Officially documented, conflicts with the English terms |
| **Office (Privacy Policy)** | "Building 7071, King Abdullah Financial District (KAFD), Al-Aqiq 2827, Riyadh 13519, Saudi Arabia" | [R004] | Officially documented, conflicts with both terms pages |
| **Office list (Subsidiaries page)** | "HQ, KAFD, Riyadh", "AMAALA, Tabuk", "The Red Sea, Hanak, Tabuk", "Jeddah, Saudi Arabia" | [R009] | Officially documented |
| **Commercial registration, unified number, VAT number** | Not verified: requires a Saudi Ministry of Commerce or Saudi Business Center extract, or a contract document | n/a | Unknown |
| **Exact registered English company name** | Not verified: the notes mark "Red Sea Global Company" as their own inference only | n/a | Inference in notes |
| **Last-updated date of Terms and Privacy Policy** | Not shown on either page | [R003] [R004] | Not publicly documented |

The redseaglobal.com terms cover "Our Website", "Our mobile application", "Any of Our social media pages" and "Any of Our other technological platforms", without naming an app [R003]. The redseaglobal.com Privacy Policy covers "our website at www.redseaglobal.com" and "the property information services we provide to you" and does not describe hotel booking data [R004]. The legal documents that govern bookings sit on other domains and are covered in Section 12.

### 3.2 Ownership and the PIF relationship

RSG and PIF describe the relationship in consistent terms. The 27 May 2018 release states "The Ministry of Commerce and Investment has registered TRSDC as a closed joint-stock company wholly owned by PIF." [R036]. The RSG home page says "As a Public Investment Fund (PIF) company, RSG is guided by Saudi Arabia’s Vision 2030" [R002]. PIF lists RSG under Our Investments, Giga-Projects, with "Launched 2018" [R030]. The board is chaired by "H.R.H. Crown Prince Mohammad bin Salman bin Abdulaziz Al Saud", shown as "Chairman of the Public Investment Fund", and John Pagano is listed as Group Chief Executive Officer [R007]. One third-party source (Argaam, 23 October 2025) also describes RSG as "owned by the Public Investment Fund" [R086].

Ownership differs at asset level. Four Seasons Resort and Residences Red Sea at Shura Island "was developed through a 50-50 joint venture between RSG and KHC [Kingdom Holding Company], with SAR 2 billion ($522 million) debt financing provided by Riyad Bank" [R066], and the Investment page lists a "SAR 2.0B Joint venture with Al‑Mutlaq Group for Jumeirah The Red Sea" [R021]. Inference recorded in the notes: "an RSG resort" does not imply that RSG is the sole owner or the operator.

Not verified: the current shareholder register, any change in ownership, and the contract type (management agreement, franchise, lease) for each branded resort. These require an RSG or PIF statement or operator disclosure.

Two cautions from the notes apply to this timeline. First, older releases on the RSG site have been retitled with the current name (the 27 May 2018 release now reads "Red Sea Global RSG (formerly known as TRSDC)") [R036], so the site cannot prove the original wording. Second, "opening" in an RSG headline can precede first guests by months: the AMAALA "opening" was announced on 11 November 2025 and first guests arrived on 15 June 2026 [R054] [R069]. The date conflicts (founding year, AMAALA integration, first guests, Shebara opening month, RSI LEED Platinum year, AlWajh reopening month, first international flights) are listed with both sources in Section 15.

### 3.5 Relationship to Saudi Vision 2030

RSG states: "As one of Saudi Arabia’s flagship companies, Red Sea Global is proud to play a central role in delivering Vision 2030, the Kingdom’s plan for economic diversification and sustainable growth." [R001]. PIF calls RSG "A cornerstone of Vision 2030" [R034]. National targets cited by RSG are "the Kingdom’s ambition to attract 150 million visitors by 2030 (updated from an original target of 100M)" [R055] and tourism at "10%" of GDP with "1.6 million jobs" [R045]. RSG's own economic figures are a target ("SAR 30–33 Billion Expected annual economic contribution to the Kingdom’s economy by 2030.") [R021] and a reported result ("Contributed SAR 24 billion in 2024 to Saudi Arabia’s Gross Domestic Product") [R020]. Not verified: the body text of the Vision 2030 website pages for The Red Sea and AMAALA, which requires a rendered browser session on vision2030.gov.sa (only a meta description was retrieved) [R087].

### 3.6 Business areas, subsidiaries and sub-brands

RSG calls itself "a vertically integrated real estate developer with a diverse portfolio spanning tourism, residential, experiences, infrastructure, transport, healthcare, and services." [R002]. It "oversees approximately 28 subsidiaries, of which 14 are public-facing" and shows "11K+ Employees" and "13 Service Areas" [R009]. Thirteen subsidiaries are named on the page [R009]. The destinations are The Red Sea, AMAALA and Thuwal Private Retreat, with "the Al Wajh Airport upgrade, and a new development in Italy" also named [R001].

| RSG unit or brand | Role as stated officially | Relevance to a resort-booking journey | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Regenerative Escapes Tourism Company (DMC)** | "manages guest logistics, bookings, and destination experiences across The Red Sea, serving as the operational arm of Visit Red Sea." Services include "24/7 B2B and B2C booking management for accommodation, transfers, and experiences" and "Call Center". "44 FTE Dedicated DMC workforce". | Runs the Visit Red Sea brand and its booking flows | [R027] |
| **Red Sea Global Hospitality Company** | "RSG’s dedicated hospitality arm". It "currently manages the Shebara and Desert Rock resorts ..., Turtle Bay Village ..., and Thuwal Private Retreat near Jeddah" | Operator of the RSG-run resorts | [R024] |
| **Red Sea Sports & Entertainment Company** | Brands "WAMA (water sports and marine activities), Galaxea (diving and underwater exploration), and Akun (land-based adventure and cultural experiences)". It "led and managed" ADRENA | Experiences | [R025] [R060] |
| **The Red Sea Air Services Company (Fly Red Sea)** | "the Kingdom’s first seaplane company", providing "air transportation services from Red Sea International and Al Wajh Airports to resorts" | Seaplane transfers | [R026] [R041] |
| **Coastal Transportation Services Company** | "integrated and sustainable mobility solutions across land, air, and marine networks" | Land and marine transfers, marinas | [R028] |
| **Red Sea Residences, AMAALA Residences** | "Launched in 2025, Red Sea Residences unites RSG’s design, development, and hospitality expertise under a single brand dedicated to bespoke homes." | Residential sales, not stays | [R029] [R012] [R014] |
| **Red Sea Health** | Launched 6 August 2025 with the Airport Clinic. The Red Sea Hospital is "Managed and operated by HMG" | Guest healthcare | [R048] [R082] |
| **Yachting Network** | "a new Marina & Yacht Club operator that brings together RSG’s marine services under a single management company". AMAALA Marina 119 berths, Shura Marina 159 berths | Marinas | [R081] [R083] |
| **Airports** | Red Sea International Airport is "operated by daa International". AlWajh International Airport was modernized by RSG | Air access | [R055] [R068] |

Inference recorded in the notes: a single guest itinerary spans several RSG entities (the DMC for bookings, RSSE for experiences, Fly Red Sea for seaplanes) plus third-party hotel operators. Not verified: whether WAMA, Galaxea and Akun are separate legal entities or trading brands, and the names of the roughly 15 subsidiaries that are not public-facing.

Technology and distribution partnerships that bear on a POC are documented only at announcement level. The stc group agreement is to "invest more than 1.2 billion SAR in expanding digital infrastructure and services across RSG’s portfolio", with scope text that includes "AI-driven services" and to "personalize guest experiences at scale" [R050]. The Bunat Ventures fund is "focused on artificial intelligence (AI)-driven startups" and lets startups "pilot and validate their technologies within RSG’s real-world operations" [R056]. Headlines only (articles not opened) name Oracle hospitality technology (13 May 2024) and an Almosafer collaboration (8 May 2024) [R023]. None of these sources describes a guest-facing booking assistant.

Not verified: the content of the 2024 Sustainability Report (hosted on Issuu, not readable with the tools used) [R088], and every certification at the certifier's own database.

### 3.8 Awards

RSG's Awards page lists 133 items [R022]. All award statements below are RSG's own reports and none was checked at the awarding body.

| Recognition | What RSG reports | Published | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MICHELIN Keys 2025** | "Shebara Resort, Six Senses Southern Dunes, Desert Rock Resort, and Nujuma, a Ritz-Carlton Reserve, were each awarded two Michelin Keys"; "The St. Regis Red Sea Resort ... earned one Michelin Key" | 9 Oct 2025 | [R052] |
| **MICHELIN Keys 2026** | "Desert Rock has been awarded Three MICHELIN Keys, becoming the first resort in Saudi Arabia to receive the highest distinction"; "Shebara has also retained Two MICHELIN Keys" | 21 Sep 2026 | [R080] |
| **Forbes Travel Guide** | "The Red Sea has become the first destination in the world to achieve a destination-wide verification from Forbes Travel Guide (FTG)", with standards "across five touch points – booking, land transfer, marine transfer, air transfer, and experiences" | 12 Feb 2026 | [R059] |
| **Other listings (headlines only, articles not opened)** | Condé Nast Traveler 2026 Hot List (The Red Sea EDITION), TIME World's Greatest Places 2025 (Shebara), Prix Versailles 2026 (Desert Rock), "RSG Hospitality Sets New Benchmark with Highest NPS Score for Guest Satisfaction in 2025" | Various | [R022] [R023] |

### 3.9 Developments in 2025 and 2026 that affect discovery and booking

By RSG's own count the open inventory grew from "five resorts" on 29 September 2025 [R050] to "nine hotels open, plus Thuwal Private Retreat" on 12 February 2026 [R059], then to "19 resorts open across its destinations: 11 at The Red Sea, seven at AMAALA, and Thuwal Private Retreat. A further eight resorts scheduled to open in the coming months." on 1 October 2026 [R083]. Only six AMAALA resorts have opening releases, so the seventh open AMAALA resort is not identified in the company notes (Section 6 records what the destination site shows). RSG published one demand figure, "occupancy reached 82% during the final 10 days of Ramadan" (2026), which is a peak-period press statement and not an annual figure [R065].

The RSG corporate AMAALA page still carries a stale FAQ: "Phase One opens in 2025/6 with eight resorts" and "Bookings will open through Visit Red Sea when the resorts begin operations", although releases report resorts open since June 2026 [R014]. Live corporate pages also contain test artefacts, such as a link to "/en/testing/demo-dec/" on the About timeline [R001].

One third-party report conflicts with RSG's account. The New Arab, carrying an AFP report on 13 February 2026 based on seven anonymous sources, said Saudi Arabia is scaling back plans for 81 Red Sea resorts by 2030 and that construction on phase two would stop at the end of 2026. The same article carries RSG's denial: "As with any large-scale, long-term destination, Phase Two is being delivered through a sequenced approach." [R084]. This article was read through a summarising fetch tool, so the wording carries paraphrase risk. Neither side's claim is independently verified.

The notes found no official announcement of a leadership change in 2025 or 2026. John Pagano is quoted as "Group CEO at Red Sea Global" in releases up to 29 September 2026 [R081] [R082]. Sultan Moraished is quoted as "Group Head of Technology and Corporate Excellence" [R056] but is not on the Leadership page [R007].

On digital channels, the media centre headlines from February 2024 to 1 October 2026 contain no release about a guest-facing mobile app, chatbot, AI concierge or WhatsApp booking channel, and a text search of the roughly 80 RSG pages and releases retrieved found no mention of "chatbot", "WhatsApp", "virtual assistant", "AI assistant" or "app store" [R023]. The apps named in official sources are the "Jewar app, a two-way communication channel for RSG to share news and opportunities with the local community" [R045] and an employee super-app (headline only) [R023]. This absence in press material does not establish that no such channel exists. Section 5 and Section 12 record what the live sites and app stores show.

## 4. Brand architecture and branding audit

Evidence class for this section: officially documented copy on RSG-controlled pages, and observed implementation (live HTML, CSS and rendered pages read on 12 October 2026 between about 18:45 and 19:05 UTC). The notes found no official brand specification of any kind. No published brand guideline, palette, type specification or logo rule was located, so every font, colour and logo statement below is observed implementation and must not be read as an RSG brand rule [R023] [R090].

### 4.1 Brand layers and official names

The notes identify four visible layers: the corporate brand, three destinations, a hospitality operator subsidiary, and experience and transport brands.

| Layer | Brand | English as used | Arabic as used | Source | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Corporate** | Red Sea Global | "Red Sea Global", short form "RSG", first mention "Red Sea Global (RSG)" | Page title "شركة البحر الأحمر الدولية", body "البحر الأحمر الدولية" | [R002] [R089] | Officially documented |
| **Parent endorsement** | PIF | "As a Public Investment Fund (PIF) company" | "إحدى شركات صندوق الاستثمارات العامة" | [R002] [R089] | Officially documented |
| **Destination** | The Red Sea | "The Red Sea" with capital T in running text ("at The Red Sea") | "البحر الأحمر", often "وجهة البحر الأحمر" | [R013] [R095] [R096] | Officially documented |
| **Consumer channel** | Visit Red Sea | Page titles end "\| Visit Red Sea". Footer "© 2026, Visit Red Sea. All rights reserved." App name "Visit Red Sea App". The rendered header logo reads "THE RED SEA" with "البحر الأحمر" beneath | Arabic home title keeps the Latin name: "وجهة البحر الأحمر \| Visit Red Sea" | [R095] [R096] [R099] | Observed implementation |
| **Destination** | AMAALA | "AMAALA" in capitals in body copy, headings, nav and footer ("© 2026, AMAALA. All rights reserved.") | "أمالا" | [R113] [R114] [R014] | Officially documented |

| **Operator subsidiary** | Red Sea Global Hospitality | "Red Sea Global Hospitality", tagline "Establishing the Kingdom's first luxury hotel operator" | "البحر الأحمر الدولية للضيافة" | [R024] [R121] | Officially documented |
| **RSG-operated resort** | Shebara | "Shebara", "Shebara Resort" in RSG nav. The island is "Sheybarah Island" | "شيبارة" | [R092] [R099] [R122] | Officially documented |

| **RSG-operated hotel** | Turtle Bay | "Turtle Bay Hotel" on Visit Red Sea and its own site, "Turtle Bay Resort" on the RSGH site | "فندق تيرتل باي" versus "منتجع ترتل باي" | [R095] [R125] [R121] | Conflicting |
| **RSG-operated resort (AMAALA)** | Daraah | H1 "Daraah Resort", page title "Book Your Stay at Darrah Resort \| AMAALA" | "منتجع دارة" | [R116] [R126] | Conflicting spelling |
| **Experience brands** | WAMA, Galaxea, AKUN | Activity brands of Red Sea Sports & Entertainment Company: above-water, below-water and inland | "واما", "جالاكسيا", "أكون" | [R025] | Officially documented |
| **Experience and venue brands** | Adrena, Laha Beach, Shura Links | "Adrena", "Laha Beach Ladies Club", "Shura Links" | "أدرينا" (Visit Red Sea) versus "أديرينا" (RSG Arabic page), "شورى لينكس" | [R096] [R025] [R112] [R128] | Officially documented, Arabic spelling conflict for Adrena |
| **Transport** | Fly Red Sea | "Fly Red Sea", legal entity "The Red Sea Air Services Company" | "فلاي رد سي" | [R026] [R009] [R130] | Officially documented |
| **Airport** | Red Sea International Airport | "Red Sea International Airport (RSI)" | "مطار البحر الأحمر الدولي (RSI)" | [R013] [R129] | Officially documented |

Official statements disagree on which resorts are RSG owned and operated. Shebara is "the first luxury resort to be fully owned and operated by Red Sea Global (RSG)" [R092], Desert Rock is the "Fifth resort to open at The Red Sea, and the third owned and operated by Red Sea Global" [R093], the hospitality subsidiary page lists "2 Owned & Operated Resorts" [R024], and the Red Sea Global Hospitality (RSGH) site lists five properties: Shebara, Thuwal Private Retreat, Desert Rock, Turtle Bay Resort and Daraah Resort [R121]. Inference in the notes: the RSG-operated set relevant to a POC is most likely those five, based on the RSGH property list.

The same AMAALA resort is named three ways on amaala.com: card "Six Senses Resort", H2 "Six Senses Resort, AMAALA", body and offers "Six Senses AMAALA" [R116] [R118]. Several sub-brands appear in more than one Arabic spelling on official pages: Shura (three spellings on visitredsea.com), Adrena, Triple Bay [R096] [R099] [R025] [R114]. Not verified: an official bilingual naming glossary (requires the RSG brand team to supply the approved English and Arabic name list) and registered trademark forms (requires a SAIP or WIPO search or RSG legal confirmation).

### 4.3 Audiences

No audience segmentation or persona statement was found. Audiences are named only in passing: "for both B2B and B2C audiences" [R027], a "Travel Trade" page [R103], investors ("Invest with us") [R002], women (Laha Beach Ladies Club, "our all-female team") [R112], families and generations [R116], staff and business visitors at Turtle Bay Hotel [R013], yacht owners [R120], residence buyers [R120] and couples through an offer name [R095]. Not verified: target markets by nationality and persona definitions (requires RSG marketing strategy or persona documents).

### 4.4 Tone of voice (observed copy, not a rule)

Guest-facing English on the destination sites is elevated and sensory with second-person address. Corporate English is formal and achievement-led. Arabic copy is rewritten and not translated literally, and it puts brand names inside quotation marks. Microcopy is plain in both languages. This characterisation is the notes author's summary of live copy [R095] [R096] [R113] [R114] [R002].

| Site | Copy type | English example | Arabic example | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| visitredsea.com | Body | "With coral reefs glowing, wild blooms swaying, and life unfolding in its purest form, here, nature is your host." | "هُنا، تستقبلك الطبيعة بسخائها؛ من توهج المرجان وتفتّح الأزهار البرية… تبدو وكأنها تبتهج برؤية ضيوفها." | [R095] [R096] |
| visitredsea.com | Consent line | "By submitting this form, you confirm that you have read and agreed to our Terms and Conditions and Privacy Policy." | "بتقديمك لهذا النموذج، فإنك تؤكد أنك قد قرأت ووافقت على الشروط والأحكام وسياسة الخصوصية." | [R110] |
| visitredsea.com | System message | "Processing action... This process may take a few seconds, do not close the screen." | Not captured | [R095] |
| visitredsea.com | Operational notice | "Surf Lagoon is temporarily closed." | "بحيرة الأمواج (Surf Lagoon) مغلقة مؤقتًا." | [R095] [R096] |
| amaala.com | Policy copy | "If you cancel between 24 and 48 hours before the scheduled start time, you will receive a 50% refund." | Not captured | [R117] |
| redseaglobal.com | Body | "RSG is guided by Saudi Arabia's Vision 2030 to create destinations and projects that deliver lasting social, environmental, and economic value" | "تستلهم \"البحر الأحمر الدولية\" رؤية السعودية 2030" | [R002] [R089] |

Observed inconsistencies in live copy: British and American spellings are mixed on visitredsea.com ("enquiry" and "travellers" beside "personalized" and "Accommodations") [R110] [R095]; uncorrected errors appear ("Seemlesly Connected", "the one of the world's largest barrier reef system", "Up to to 5 attendees") [R105] [R095] [R107]; Arabic on visitredsea.com uses Western digits in most places and Arabic-Indic digits in one line [R096]. Inference in the notes: English and Arabic taglines often carry different ideas, so on-brand Arabic cannot be produced by translating English lines. Not verified: any tone-of-voice guideline, glossary or style guide (requires RSG to supply guidance in English and Arabic). Transactional copy (confirmation page, confirmation email, chat greetings) was not observed because no booking was made and the chat was not opened.

The destination platforms use "Book" as the primary action and the RSG-operated resort sites use "Check Availability". "Enquire" appears only for residences [R109].

### 4.6 Naming conventions for rooms, offers and experiences

Operator resorts at The Red Sea mostly follow "[Operator brand] The Red Sea". At AMAALA the pattern is "[Operator brand] Resort" on cards and "[Operator brand] AMAALA" in copy [R095] [R113]. Villas follow "[Bedroom count] Bedroom [Setting] [Tier] Villa". Shebara's eight villa names (for example "One Bedroom Overwater Villa", "Four Bedroom Beach Royal Villa") are identical on visitredsea.com and shebara.sa [R099] [R122]. Desert Rock is not consistent: visitredsea.com lists names such as "One bedroom Wadi Villa" and "One Bedroom Mountain Crevice Villa" [R100], while desertrock.sa hyphenates and adds a "Mountain View Villas" category that the destination site does not list [R123]. Turtle Bay Hotel and Daraah Resort use hotel-style names ("Deluxe King Room", "Family Room") [R101] [R116]. Room cards on visitredsea.com order attributes as occupancy, bedrooms, bed type, setting, area, pool: "2 Adults + 1 Child + 1 Infant 1 Bedroom 1 King Bed Overwater Villa 188 sqm Private Pool" [R099].

Offers at The Red Sea are named by theme ("Multi-Resort Escape Offer", "The Red Sea & AlUla Expedition", "Red Sea Wellness Escape", "Romantic Getaway at The Red Sea", "Fly Red Sea Seaplane Special Offers", "Adrena Day Pass"). The nav says "Promotions", the URL is /offers-and-packages, the H2 says "OFFERS" and the listing says "4 promotions" [R095] [R108]. AMAALA offers are "AMAALA Resorts Opening Offers" with sub-offers for Four Seasons, Six Senses and Rosewood [R118]. The RSSE activity brands are not used as category labels on visitredsea.com ("Water Sports", "Adventure Sports"), while amaala.com uses the brand in copy ("WAMA-led activities", "WAMA Cancellation Policy") [R095] [R117]. Rate text follows the form "All room rates, charges and fees are in Saudi Arabian Riyal (SAR) and are subject to 5% Municipality tax (MT) and 15% Value added tax (VAT)" on the Shebara page of visitredsea.com [R099]. Inference in the notes: room and villa names are a controlled list per resort and should be loaded from data. Not verified: an official room-type master list with Arabic equivalents (requires an RSG Hospitality inventory or PMS room-type export).

The full token lists are in the brand notes file. The CSS does not prove which tokens are primary brand colours or how they may be combined (inference in notes). Not verified: official palettes, colour ratios, accessibility pairings, typeface licences and permitted fallbacks (requires RSG brand guidelines per brand and font licence confirmation). Fonts and colours of thuwalretreat.sa, turtlebayhotel.sa, tbha-hotel.com, flyredsea.com, rsiairport.sa and shuralinks.com were not extracted.

Each site references separate light and dark or English and Arabic logo files. RSG has a distinct Arabic header logo (red-sea-ar.svg) [R089]. Visit Red Sea uses "VRS Logo-light.svg" and "VRS Logo-dark.svg" in both languages [R095] [R096]. AMAALA uses one "AMAALA_Bilingual_Logotype_Pt_White_RGB.svg" in both languages [R113] [R114]. The "A PIF company" endorsement mark (apifcompany.svg) appears in the RSG and RSGH footers [R002] [R121]. Operator logos hosted by the destination are destination-specific lockups, for example "EDITION_Logo_TheRedSea-white.svg" [R095]. Logo files were not opened or compared visually, apart from two described browser views, so variant descriptions rely on file names and alt text. Several alt texts are wrong or placeholders: the PIF mark's alt is "Airport" [R002] and the AMAALA footer logo's alt is "Visit Red Sea" [R113]. Not verified: clear space, minimum size, co-branding and endorsement rules (requires RSG and operator brand guidelines).

| Site | `<html>` attributes on the Arabic page | Note | Source |

| visitredsea.com/ar | `lang="ar" dir="rtl"` | 166 `[dir=rtl]` selectors, same logo files as English | [R096] [R097] |

English and Arabic pages state different facts in several places. The English press boilerplate says AMAALA "welcomed its first guests in 2026" while the Arabic boilerplate on the same release says AMAALA will open in 2025 [R079]. The RSG AMAALA page gives "4,200km2" and "9 Hotels" in English and "4,155 كيلومتر مربع" and "7 منتجعات" in Arabic [R014]. The Daraah Resort description on amaala.com says "Located around 20–30 minutes north of Triple Bay" in English and places the resort in Triple Bay in Arabic [R116]. Wind speed on the AMAALA home is "mph" in English and km/h in Arabic with the same figures [R113] [R114]. Mirrored layout was inferred from attributes and CSS rules and was not confirmed by side-by-side screenshots. The Arabic experience of the booking flow, the chat widget and the app was not observed in the brand workstream.

### 4.9 Public brand assets and licence position

No public brand guidelines, press kit or media kit were found for RSG, The Red Sea or AMAALA. Nine guessed brand and press URLs on redseaglobal.com returned 404 [R023]. The closest items are a Travel Trade toolkit page on visitredsea.com with four downloads ("The Red Sea Factsheets", "Media Pitches", "The Red Sea Boilerplate", "The Red Sea Images") whose host did not resolve from the research machine, so the files were not downloaded [R103], ten corporate PDFs under media centre Resources, and an "Image Gallery" with no stated licence [R023].

The terms on redseaglobal.com, visitredsea.com and amaala.com share this wording: "RSG owns (and where applicable is the licensee of) all Intellectual Property Rights in the Content. All rights, including those not expressly granted, in and to the Platforms and the Content accessible thereon, are reserved by RSG." [R003] [R104] [R119]. The licence granted is for "personal and non-commercial use", and "Any other use of Content on Our Platforms, including but not limited to the modification, distribution, transmission, performance, broadcast, publication, uploading, licensing is expressly prohibited." [R104]. The trademark carve-out reads: "nothing on Our Platforms shall be construed as granting, by implication, estoppel, or otherwise, any license or right to use any trademark, logo displayed on the Platforms without the owner's prior written permission." [R003]. These are short fragments and must be re-read at source before legal use. Inference in the notes: reusing live-site copy, images, logos, fonts or layout in a POC shown outside personal and non-commercial use is outside the stated licence, so RSG would need to supply or approve the assets in writing.

### 4.10 Which brand governs each journey stage

| Path | Stage | Governing brand as observed | Evidence | Source | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Destination platform | Discovery and resort page | Destination brand ("The Red Sea" lockup, "Visit Red Sea" titles, AMAALA logotype) with the resort's own logo on the resort page | Header logo, titles, footer copyright | [R095] [R113] [R099] | Observed implementation |
| A | Booking entry and engine | Destination-branded widget and on-domain /booking/ routes on both visitredsea.com and amaala.com | Widget opened, route strings found in page scripts | [R099] [R116] | Observed implementation |

| C | Flights | Flight listings link to airline sites | Link | [R106] | Observed implementation |

amaala.com and visitredsea.com share a stylesheet file name, the /content/dam/red-sea/ asset root, the same /booking/ route set, the same chat loader path and the same phone number [R095] [R113]. Inference in the notes: one codebase appears to serve The Red Sea, AMAALA and Shura Links with brand themes swapped. A second inference in the notes: the same RSG-operated resort can be booked through two engines with two brand wrappers, and which path the guest used decides which brand should speak in confirmation and support. That is a decision for RSG and is not established by public evidence.

### 4.11 Brand items that need documents or approval from RSG

The brand notes list fifteen items. In summary: brand guidelines for each brand an agent would speak for; a written decision on which brand the agent represents at each stage on each path; an approved English and Arabic name list including the rule for "The Red Sea" versus "Visit Red Sea" and the capitalisation of AMAALA; tone-of-voice guidance in both languages; official palettes and type specifications; font licences for Questa Grande, Paralucent, Fedra and the AMAALA typefaces; logo files with written permission; permission and guidelines for third-party operator marks; a licence for copy, imagery and video; the Travel Trade toolkit contents; room and villa master data in both languages; approved CTA vocabulary; current confirmation page, email and chat scripts; whether WAMA, Galaxea and AKUN are guest-facing names; and clarification of the conflicts in Section 15. These items are carried into Sections 17 and 18.

## 5. Digital ecosystem and website map

Evidence class for this section: directly observed in the live interface on 12 October 2026 between 18:45 and 19:13 UTC at a 1024x768 desktop viewport (375x812 for the mobile pass), plus markup read from fetched HTML or the DOM without being displayed, plus official store listings. Where a statement comes from fetched markup and not from a rendered screen, the text says so.

### 5.1 The three RSG sites are two different builds

redseaglobal.com is a corporate site with a mega menu, no booking and no account [R002]. visitredsea.com and amaala.com share one guest platform with the same header pattern (language and currency, Map, Account, BOOK) and a flat one-level menu [R095] [R113]. Inference in the notes: the two guest sites run on one shared platform, because they have identical header components, an identical booking URL pattern, an identical chat widget path (/chat/widgetloader.html) and the amaala.com sitemap is generated from the same content repository [R151].

| Site | Role | Top-level navigation (English) | Arabic | Booking | Account | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **redseaglobal.com** | Corporate | Who we are, Our Business, Our Portfolio, Positive Impact, Invest, Join Us, Media Center | Mirrors English one for one, `lang="ar"`, `dir="rtl"` | None. Links out only | None | [R002] [R089] |
| **visitredsea.com** | Guest platform for The Red Sea | RESORTS, EXPERIENCES, DINING, PROMOTIONS, SHURA ISLAND, PLAN YOUR TRIP. Header: "English / SAR", Map, Account, BOOK | المنتجعات, التجارب, المطاعم, عروض حصرية, جزيرة شُورى, خطط رحلتك | On-site widget and /booking/ flow | Email and password drawer | [R095] [R096] |
| **amaala.com** | Guest platform for AMAALA | RESORTS, EXPERIENCES, AMAALA YACHT CLUB, PLAN YOUR TRIP. Header: "English / SAR", account icon, map icon, "Book" | المنتجعات, التجارب, نادي أمالا لليخوت, خطط لرحلتك | Same widget and flow | Same drawer | [R113] [R114] |

Second-level navigation on visitredsea.com exists only as in-page tab bars: Experiences has Water Sports, Adventure Sports, Nature, Adrena, Laha Beach, Shura Links, and Plan Your Trip has Airport & Flights, Weather & Proximity, Getting to & Around [R140] [R106]. The visitredsea.com footer lists Residences, Track My Luggage, Travel Trade, Visit Red Sea App, phone +966 11 222 2222, info@visitredsea.com, app store badges, Terms & Conditions, Privacy Policy, Privacy Center, "MOT license: 73104512" and "CR: 1010916245" [R095]. The amaala.com footer has Offers, Dining, Residences, the same phone number and info@amaala.com, and has no app badges, no Privacy Center, no Track My Luggage and no MOT or CR line [R113].

### 5.4 Discovery features

Discovery is card and carousel based. visitredsea.com /en/resorts shows "13 RESORTS", six cards and a "7 MORE RESORTS" button, with tags (Wellness, Beachfront, Over-water, Rocky Mountains, Dune). Ten cards have a BOOK button. Raffles, Grand Hyatt and InterContinental cards have none [R098]. The listing's only filter, "I'm looking for a [breathtaking]", offered a single option when opened [R098]. The home page has a resort carousel with category tabs [R095]. /en/map is a full-page Mapbox map with zoom controls and no search or category filter at load [R146]. The guest sites have no header search. The only free-text search is "Search experiences and activities" inside the booking widget [R095]. amaala.com /en/resorts shows "9 resorts" with no filter control. Seven have BOOK and two are labelled "Opening Soon" (Clinique La Prairie Health Resort, The Ritz-Carlton Resort) [R148] [R113]. No comparison tool for resorts, rooms or prices was found on any page visited [R098]. Inference in the notes: a visitor on visitredsea.com does not see AMAALA resorts in the listing or the widget, and the reverse is also true.

Resort pages are rich on rooms, amenities, dining, access and policies but show no prices and no availability. The Shebara page carries eight villa cards, a 28-item amenity list, dining, "Getting To Shebara Resort", offers and a "Reservation Policy" accordion that includes an Accessibility Information panel [R099]. Prices first appear on the booking results screen as "Average price per night excluding taxes" [R139]. Resort pages have no FAQ block. A general booking FAQ with 12 questions exists at /en/faqs-booking (answers were read from fetched HTML because the accordions were collapsed) [R144].

The booking widget has three tabs. Resorts: "Where would you like to go?", Guests (default "2 Adults, 0 Children"), "Check in — Check out", Nights, "ADD ANOTHER RESORT", SEARCH. Experiences: "What do you want to do?", "Where are you staying?", "When?". Transportation: From, To, "When?", Guest (default "1 Adult") [R095]. The widget's resort picker lists 11 resorts and omits Raffles and Grand Hyatt [R095]. The date picker disables today and past dates and shows "For bookings under 24 hours, please chat with us directly." [R099]. The Transportation "From" list mixes three airports with about 70 locations that include operational sites such as "RSG Nursery" and "Tamimi Main Market" [R095].

### 5.6 Trip planning, offers, experiences and luggage tools

The Plan Your Trip section has a flight information widget (four flights listed on the day: QR1226 DOH, EY621 AUH, SV1569 RUH, FZ927 DXB) and practical notes [R106]. Whether the flight board is live data or scheduled data was not established. The offers listing shows "4 PROMOTIONS" [R141]. The Multi-Resort Escape Offer page was still live and promoted on 12 October 2026 although its own terms say "Offer valid only between 2nd Jul to 20th Sep 2026". It prices the package at "17,250 SAR for 2 Guests" and its only action is "CONTACT US" [R141]. Experience cards list age, group size and conditions and show no prices [R140]. A "REQUEST A QUOTE" three-step modal exists. Its 14 answer options rendered as empty pills on the one page tested [R140]. Track My Luggage offers BAG TAGS and PNR tabs with one field and a TRACK MY LUGGAGE button [R142].

### 5.10 Language and currency

All three sites offer English and Arabic. On visitredsea.com the "English / SAR" button opens a "Settings" modal with a Language dropdown (عربى, English) and a Currency dropdown (CNY, EUR, GBP, RUB, SAR, USD) [R099]. Choosing Arabic on /en/resorts/shebara navigated to /ar/resorts/shebara, the same page [R099]. amaala.com has the same control [R113]. redseaglobal.com has a language menu and no currency control [R135]. Currency switching was not applied in this workstream. Section 9 records what the booking workstream saw when it changed currency.

Differences between English and Arabic versions on the three main sites are mostly at link or label level. The redseaglobal.com English footer "AMAALA" link goes to the guest site while the Arabic one goes to the corporate page [R002] [R089]. The Arabic "Privacy Center" links on redseaglobal.com and visitredsea.com point to the English page [R089] [R099]. On visitredsea.com "PROMOTIONS" becomes "عروض حصرية" and room "BOOK" becomes "احجز الآن" [R096] [R099]. Arabic forms, Arabic booking results and Arabic mobile layouts were not inspected in this workstream.

## 6. Destination and resort inventory

Evidence class for this section: officially documented statements on RSG and operator pages and press releases, and live observation of booking buttons, widget contents and embedded page data on 12 October 2026. Three operator sources (Rosewood AMAALA, Jayasom, The Ritz-Carlton, AMAALA) and two Red Sea sources (SLS text, the Four Seasons press release) were read through a summarising fetch tool, so wording from them carries paraphrase risk. The inventory workstreams ran no availability search. Whether a date is actually sellable is covered in Section 9.

### 6.1 How the groups are defined

The groups below are kept apart because "announced", "opening soon", "reservable" and "open" are different facts with different evidence.

| Group | Definition used in this report | Count |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Operating and bookable | A dated official opening statement exists and a live booking entry was observed | 17 (11 at The Red Sea, 6 at AMAALA) |
| 2. Operating, enquiry only | Official statement that the property is open, with no online booking | 1 (Thuwal Private Retreat) |
| 3. Bookable, operating status not explicitly confirmed | Booking buttons are live but no dated opening statement was found | 1 (Daraah Resort) |
| 4. Not open per official labels, reservation entry or rate page published | An official label says "Coming Soon" or "Opening Soon", and a reservation entry point or full rate and policy page exists | 4 (Grand Hyatt, Raffles, Fairmont, The Ritz-Carlton AMAALA) |
| 5. Announced, no reservation entry found | Official "Coming Soon" or "Opening Soon" label and no booking entry | 4 (Rosewood Red Sea, Jumeirah, Faena, Clinique La Prairie) |
| 6. Implied but unnamed | Counted in an official total but not named in any page opened | 1 |

No property is placed in an "under development" group. None of the pages opened describes construction status for a named resort. RSG's wording for the six Shura resorts that are not open is "Coming Soon" [R170] and "six additional resorts scheduled to open on Shura Island in the coming months" [R080].

RSG's official counts are "11 at The Red Sea" and "seven at AMAALA" open on 1 October 2026 [R083] [R083]. No single RSG source itemises the 11 or the seven. The mapping of the 11 to the Group 1 Red Sea rows is an inference in the notes, based on six pre-Shura openings named in the RSG overview [R013], five Shura hotels named as operational on 9 July 2026 [R071] and the same 11 being the only resorts selectable in the booking widget [R099]. For AMAALA, six resorts have dated opening releases, so the seventh is not identified by an official source. The notes infer that it is Daraah Resort. That remains an inference.

### 6.2 Inventory by status group

**1. Operating and bookable**

Not verified | [R198] [R218] |

### 6.5 Thuwal Private Retreat

Thuwal Private Retreat "operates as a buy-out-only property, welcoming just one group at a time" [R015]. The enquiry page says "Every booking at Thuwal Private Retreat is completely bespoke. The island rate varies according to the season and day of the week." and "Rates include accommodation for up to twelve adults and three children, full-board meals (breakfast, lunch, afternoon tea, soft drinks, cocktails and dinner), and a range of on-island activities." [R127]. Access is "via a 45-minute chartered yacht from King Abdullah University of Science and Technology (KAUST) North Marina in northern Jeddah" [R127]. The accessibility statement reads "due to the nature of the island, our transportation options and facilities may not be equipped to safely and comfortably accommodate all physical disabilities." [R127]. Contact: "+966 9200 11099" and "Inquiries.Thuwal@RedSeaGlobalHospitality.com" [R127]. The enquiry form (Enquiry Type, First Name, Last Name, Email, Phone Number, message) was not submitted. Not verified: nearest airport, minimum stay, rates and cancellation terms.

### 6.7 Leads rejected or reassigned

Thuwal Private Retreat is not part of The Red Sea destination. RSG presents it as a separate destination near Jeddah [R015] [R065]. No property with "wadi" in its name exists in any source opened [R159] [R100]. Rosewood, Four Seasons and Six Senses each have one property at The Red Sea and a separate property at AMAALA, and these must not be merged [R147]. Nujuma belongs to The Red Sea, not AMAALA [R023] [R130]. Outside The Red Sea and AMAALA, the only RSG destination on the portfolio pages is Thuwal Private Retreat, and the 2025 to 2026 news list contains no new destination announcement [R012] [R023].

## 7. UX findings with supporting observations

Evidence class for this section: directly observed in the live interface unless a row says "fetched HTML" or "DOM". Findings are spot checks. No screen reader, automated audit tool or contrast measurement was used, and no accessibility compliance conclusion is drawn. Page load performance was not measured.

Both URLs answered HTTP 200 to a HEAD request without authentication | [R148] [R150] [R156] | Observed live. Status check from fetched HTML. UAT site not browsed |
| U17 | Corporate form legal links are placeholders | Contact form links point to "/editor.html/content/redsea-rsc/en/testing/contact-us-test.html#". Vendor form links point to "https://www.google.com/" | [R132] [R133] | Fetched HTML and observed live |
| U18 | Privacy policy URL is misspelled and some legal links are dead | /en/privacy-policy redirects to /en/private-policy. The residential form links to /en/terms-and-conditions.html and /en/private-policy.html, both 404 | [R109] | Observed live. 404s from fetched HTML |

Inference in the notes: release hygiene between authoring, UAT and production is weak on both platforms, and a publicly reachable UAT site for the guest platform is a security and brand exposure worth raising with RSG.

## 8. Account creation and authentication analysis

No account was created and no credentials were entered on any site. Every statement in this section describes screens that can be reached without submitting anything, or text published by the site owner.

### 8.1 Account entry on RSG-owned sites (visitredsea.com and amaala.com)

Evidence class: directly observed in the live interface on 12 October 2026, 18:49 to 19:05 UTC.

| Element | What was observed | Source | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Entry points** | "Account" in the visitredsea.com desktop header (an anchor with no href), "Log In or Sign Up" at the top of the mobile burger menu, "Account" in the booking flow header. On amaala.com the desktop entry is an unlabeled person icon | [R095] [R113] | Observed live |
| **Hosting** | The drawer opens over the current page and the URL stays on www.visitredsea.com or www.amaala.com. No redirect to a separate identity domain happened up to the first screen. A hidden reCAPTCHA Enterprise frame is loaded on the page | [R095] | Observed live |
| **Log in view** | Title "Log in". "Email *" (type email, required). "Password *" (type password, required) with a "Show password" toggle. "Forgot password?". CONTINUE. "Don't have an account? Register" | [R095] | Observed live |
| **Log in view on amaala.com** | Adds "* You can use Red Sea account to log in if you have one." and "By continuing you confirm that you have read & agreed to our Terms and Conditions and Privacy Policy." | [R113] | Observed live |
| **Register view, step 1** | Title "Register". One field "Email *" (required, autocomplete off). Helper text "Use the email from your booking". CONTINUE. "Already have an account? Log in". No name, phone, password, country-code picker, terms checkbox or marketing checkbox on this step | [R095] | Observed live |
| **Password recovery** | "Forgot your password? Please enter your email address to restore your password." with "Email *" and CONTINUE | [R095] | Observed live |
| **Social sign-in** | None. No Google, Apple or other provider button in the Log in, Register or Forgot password views | [R095] [R113] | Observed live |
| **Password rules** | None shown on the Log in view. The Register view has no password field at step 1 | [R095] | Observed live |
| **Arabic drawer** | Opens mirrored with translated labels ("تسجيل الدخول", "البريد الإلكتروني *", "كلمة المرور *"). Two control names stay English in the markup ("Close", "Show password") | [R096] | Observed live |
| **"My Trips"** | The booking FAQ says a guest can request a change or cancellation "through your account in the 'My Trips' section". The app page describes "a dedicated 'My Trips' section" | [R144] [R105] | Officially documented (FAQ from fetched HTML) |
| **Loyalty** | No loyalty page, tier or enrollment form was found. The only reference is the booking screen banner "Your rewards are secured. Complete your booking and register — exclusive benefits will be ready soon." | [R139] | Observed live |
| **redseaglobal.com** | No login, register, account or profile control on any page visited | [R002] | Observed live |

Inferences recorded in the notes, kept as inferences: the account is shared across The Red Sea and AMAALA (stated on amaala.com); the register hint "Use the email from your booking" suggests the web account is designed to attach to an existing reservation, with booking first and registration second; a loyalty or benefits scheme is planned but not launched, based on "will be ready soon".

Not verified: everything after the first Register step (name and phone fields, password rules, email or phone one-time code, terms and marketing consent). This requires submitting an email address, which was out of bounds. Not verified: the logged-in area (profile, My Trips, saved guests, payment methods), which requires an account. Not verified: which API host and identity service handle authentication, which requires watching the network on a real login.

On every route reached, the guest-details page could be opened without signing in. That shows guest checkout is offered up to that page. It does not show that a booking completes without an account, because nothing was submitted.

Steps 0 to 3 need no login | Guest form label "E-Mail (We'll send a verification code) *". In the "FOR SOMEONE ELSE" variant the booker email carries that wording. Whether the code is mandatory, and when it is sent, is not verified | Banner on every step: "Your rewards are secured. Complete your booking and register, exclusive benefits will be ready soon." | [R223] [R219] | Observed live |
| R1, bot check | A reCAPTCHA response field (g-recaptcha-response) is present on the guest-information form and the Track My Luggage form. No visible challenge appeared | Not applicable | Not applicable | Not applicable | [R142] [R223] | Observed live (markup) |
| R2, RSGH engine | "Log In" control opens a modal "Login to your account to get access to your exclusive member rates." with "Email Address *" and "Password *" | Yes | None indicated | "exclusive member rates" (not viewed) | [R230] | Observed live |
| Marriott | "Sign in for Faster Booking" on the guest page | Yes | None indicated | "Join Marriott Bonvoy now for free." was already checked on load. The marketing email checkbox was also already checked. Member rate 6,370 vs non-member 6,500 per night on the St. Regis Dune Villa | [R239] | Observed live |
| IHG | "Sign in" in the header | Yes, reached with the public rate | None indicated | Switch "Apply IHG One Rewards Discount" is ON by default, so list prices show the member price (2,231 vs 2,348 SAR) | [R240] | Observed live |
| Six Senses | LOGIN in the header | Yes, for the public Best Flexible Rate | None indicated | "Member Exclusive Best Flex" carries "Not a member yet? Join for free to book this rate." | [R241] | Observed live |

Inference in the notes: a guest who books on R1 without an account probably has to register afterwards with the booking email to see "My Trips" and request changes. The Register helper text "Use the email from your booking" supports this, but it was not tested.

### 8.3 What remains unverified about accounts and verification

Not verified: whether the R1 email verification code is mandatory for guest checkout (requires entering a real test email in an authorised test). Not verified: whether R1 and R2 accounts are the same identity system. Not verified: whether choosing a member-labelled rate on any operator engine forces sign-in or enrollment before the guest page (not tested, because it would require starting enrollment). Not verified: points redemption flows. Not verified: any one-time password, 3-D Secure or CAPTCHA challenge after submit, on any route. Loyalty enrollment on operator engines is a separate act from account creation on RSG sites, and from the reservation itself. The notes record no case where an account was required to reach the guest-details page.

## 9. Booking journeys with property-specific differences

Evidence class for this section: directly observed in the live interface on 12 October 2026 between 18:45 and 19:23 UTC, plus API output obtained by replaying the destination site's own search request (always labelled as API output, never as a rendered screen), plus static FAQ and policy text. No name, email, phone, card or other identity data was typed anywhere. No account was created, no sign-in attempted, no terms box ticked, and no submit, confirm, pay or enquiry button was pressed. Every route below therefore stops before a reservation exists.

Test scenarios used by the booking workstreams: A = 2 adults, 1 room, 12 to 15 January 2027. B = 2 adults and 2 children (5 and 10), 1 room, 14 to 18 March 2027. C = 4 adults, 2 rooms, 12 to 15 January 2027. Alternative dates were used where A, B or C returned nothing: 10 to 13 November 2026 (A-alt and C-alt), 15 to 19 November 2026 (B-alt), and 8 to 11 December 2026 (Jayasom only).

### 9.1 Finding that reshapes the journey map

visitredsea.com and amaala.com do not hand guests off to operator booking engines. Their BOOK buttons open RSG's own widget and the search stays on the RSG domain, including for Marriott, IHG, Hyatt, Accor, Four Seasons, Rosewood, Equinox and Nammos properties [R102] [R098] [R113]. None of the 13 visitredsea.com resort pages contains a link to marriott.com, ihg.com, hyatt.com, accor.com, fourseasons.com, sixsenses.com, slshotels.com or raffles.com (fetched HTML) [R098]. The only external booking link found on either RSG site is Jayasom, from amaala.com to jayasom.com/booking, with no dates or guests carried [R113] [R148]. Operator engines are a parallel direct channel reached from the operator's own website.

One same-date comparison exists. For the St. Regis Red Sea Resort, scenario A, RSG's engine showed One Bedroom Dune Villa at 5,525.00, 6,500.00 and 7,700.00 SAR ("Average price per night excluding taxes") [R238], and marriott.com showed the same three figures as its non-member and package rates for the same villa and dates [R239]. Inference in the notes: RSG is fed the operator's public rate plans and does not show member rates. Who is merchant of record on RSG-channel bookings for operator resorts, and how those bookings appear in operator systems, is not verified by the booking workstreams. Section 12 records what the published booking terms say about the seller.

### 9.2 Route overview and the exact boundary reached on each

| Route | Channel and domain | Properties covered | Platform evidence (no vendor confirmed) | Account needed to reach the boundary | Exact boundary reached | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | Destination-site engine, www.visitredsea.com, /en/booking/ | 11 resorts in the widget (RSG-operated and third-party). Walked for Shebara. Step 1 seen for Desert Rock and St. Regis. Each RSG-operated resort therefore has two live booking routes that do not share a session, and they use different hotel identifiers (R1: Shebara 1035, Desert Rock 1053, Turtle Bay Hotel 1069. R2: Shebara 62780, Turtle Bay Hotel 62781, Desert Rock 62782) [R225] [R227]. Inference in the notes: the "p3hotels" and "p3custom" path names on R2 point to a white-labelled third-party booking engine product. That is a reading of file names and needs contractual or vendor confirmation. Not verified: the vendor or system behind either RSG engine, and the upstream reservation, property-management or payment systems.

### 9.4 Differences between the two RSG-run routes for the same resorts

| Topic | R1, destination-site engine | R2, RSGH brand-site engine | Source | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Availability for 12 to 15 Jan 2027** | Nothing returned for Shebara, Desert Rock and Turtle Bay Hotel (UI for Shebara, API output for all three) | Bookable rates returned for Desert Rock and Turtle Bay Hotel. The Shebara calendar marks 12 to 15 Jan as "Available date" | [R220] [R225] [R228] [R235] [R234] | Conflicting between channels |
| **Availability for 14 to 18 Mar 2027** | Nothing returned for Shebara | Two villa types returned for Shebara | [R225] [R231] | Conflicting between channels |
| **Tax presentation** | Breakdown shows one line "VAT" equal to 15% of the accommodation subtotal (28,665.00 + 4,299.75 = 32,964.75) and no municipality line | Room price, "Municipality Fee - 5%" and "VAT - Room 15%" (25,100 + 1,255 + 3,953.25 = 30,308.25) | [R219] [R230] | Conflicting presentation |
| **Rate plan names** | "Villa Flat Rate", "SHP - Stay 3 Nights Or More And Enjoy 20% Off" | "Villa Rate Including Breakfast", "Plan Ahead and Save 25%", "Romantic Couple's Getaway" | [R219] [R228] | Observed live |
| **Payment wording before submit** | Not shown before step 05 | "Don't worry, you won't be charged! Your card is for guarantee purposes only." "Due now 0", "Pay on arrival" | [R223] [R230] | Observed live |
| **Transfers** | For a by-plane guest to Shebara the engine adds "recommended" transfers itself, with no skip control on that tile | Transfers are an optional add-on with "SKIP THIS STEP" | [R221] [R229] | Observed live |
| **Shebara transfer price basis** | 1,191.40 per leg as the total for 2 adults | Round trip 1,191.40 "per person" | [R221] [R232] | Conflicting unit basis |
| **Rooms** | No rooms control in the widget. Extra units are added by a quantity stepper, each priced for the full party | True per-room occupancy ("CHOOSE ROOM 1 / 2 ADULTS") | [R219] [R228] | Observed live |
| **Children** | Child ages asked. A search with two children and blank ages was accepted and the children were dropped without a message | Child ages never asked. The engine wrote age 5 for both children into its links | [R237] [R231] | Observed live |
| **Promo code** | No promo, voucher or corporate code field on the widget or steps 1 to 4 | "Add A Code" with "Group Code", "Promotion Code", "Corporate Rate" | [R095] [R227] | Observed live |
| **Deep links** | An accommodation-list URL without the client-generated searchId lost the dates and bounced to an empty state | Rates, add-ons and guest details pages all opened directly from their URLs | [R220] [R229] | Observed live |
| **Sold-out handling** | "Shebara has no availability for the selected dates. We've curated other exclusive alternatives matching your preferences." with alternative resorts | Redirect to a calendar with each date marked "Available date" plus a price or "Not Available date" | [R220] [R234] | Observed live |
| **Stock messaging** | No stock count in the UI (the API returns availableRooms) | "Only 3 villas left!" on several Desert Rock villa types | [R225] [R228] | Observed live and API output |
| **Identity documents** | No nationality, date of birth, ID or passport field at step 4 | No such field on the form, but Shebara transfer add-on text asks guests to email "A copy of passports and visa or ID" and flight details to the concierge | [R223] [R232] | Observed live |

Inference in the notes: R1's RSG-operated hotels appear to have rates loaded only to the end of 2026 at the time of testing, because every 2027 probe for Shebara, Desert Rock, Turtle Bay and Daraah was empty while third-party resorts on the same engine returned January 2027 results. This is a pattern in the data, not a statement from RSG. Not verified: the cause, which requires confirmation from the RSG distribution or revenue team. No same-date, same-room price comparison between R1 and R2 exists in the notes, because the date sets observed did not overlap.

Other R1 behaviour recorded once each: setting USD in Settings did not change booking prices, the API returned SAR when asked for USD, and the session key booking_locked_currency held "SAR" (an unfinished SAR booking existed in the same tab, so a clean-session test is still needed) [R237] [R225]. Replacing /en/ with /ar/ rendered step 1 right-to-left with Arabic labels while rate plan names stayed in English [R236]. Browser back from step 4 to step 2 kept selections and totals [R221]. At step 2, choosing RSIA made the engine fill "Airline" with "Saudia" and "Flight Number" with "SV1569" by itself [R221]. The transfer "CANCELLATION DETAILS" dialog returned "Cancellation policy details are currently unavailable, Please try again later." [R221]. No booking, cart or hold request was sent to the booking API between step 1 and the guest-information page. Cart state is kept in browser sessionStorage [R223]. The FAQ says "You will be warned before exiting", and no exit warning appeared in the session [R144].

The booking FAQ on visitredsea.com states channel rules for R1: "bookings must be made at least one day in advance. Same-day bookings are not allowed. If you need to make a booking for the same day, please use the live assistant chat." and "You can make a booking up to 6 months in advance." [R144]. Several steps inside R1 are request-based: BY YACHT ("We will reach out to you before your arrival to finalize the arrangements."), BY CAR ("We will contact to provide the parking details."), and experiences ("Our customer care team will contact you to confirm the exact time of your activity.") [R221] [R222].

### 9.5 Differences between operator engines

| Topic | What was observed | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **State handling** | Marriott, IHG, Six Senses and Four Seasons guest pages have no query parameters (inference in notes: state lives in a server session). Raffles and SynXis carry state in the URL. SLS uses a booking UUID | [R239] [R240] [R241] [R244] [R182] [R245] [R243] |
| **Default currency** | SAR on Marriott, IHG, SLS, Four Seasons and SynXis. USD on Raffles with a SAR equivalent only in the cart. Six Senses defaulted to EGP by visitor location and re-priced to SAR in place | [R239] [R240] [R243] [R244] [R245] [R182] [R241] |
| **Children** | Marriott asks each child's age (<1 to 17) and returned only two-bedroom villas at St. Regis. Four Seasons accepted two children with no ages and switched standard rooms to "Please Call". SynXis kept child ages in the URL. Raffles labels a child as "until 12 years old". The IHG deep link with children did not render | [R239] [R244] [R245] [R182] [R240] |
| **Two rooms** | Marriott (EDITION) accepted two rooms and showed "2 Rooms, 2 Guests" with prices per room. Not tested elsewhere | [R239] |
| **Arabic** | Six Senses /ar/ results rendered right-to-left with Arabic labels and English room names. Marriott lists Arabic in its language dialog. Not opened on other engines | [R241] [R239] |
| **Unavailable dates** | Jayasom showed a "No availability" modal for scenario A. Six Senses shows per-day states ("Available", "Minimum stay", "No arrival") before search. No other engine returned zero availability for the test dates | [R248] [R241] |
| **Extras step** | IHG offers paid room upgrades and points. Six Senses offers transfers with flight fields. Rosewood offers four airport transfer items. Raffles offers a room upgrade | [R240] [R241] [R245] [R182] |
| **Card capture** | Marriott (gr4vy iframes) and SLS (Stripe iframes) use hosted fields. Six Senses, Four Seasons and Rosewood use native inputs. Four Seasons, Rosewood and IHG showed no CVV field at this stage. Jayasom shows no card fields before "CONFIRM BOOKING" | [R239] [R243] [R241] [R244] [R245] [R240] [R248] |

Back navigation, refresh and session expiry were not systematically tested on the operator engines. Field validation messages were not triggered anywhere, because nothing was typed and nothing was submitted.

### 9.6 Side effects of rate selection that must be disclosed

On marriott.com, clicking "Select" on a rate opened the guest page with the banner "Room(s) held for 14:54", a timed hold that was not labelled on the button. The page was read and left, the hold was left to expire, and no rate was selected on any other Marriott property [R239]. On booking.slshotels.com, "Select this rate" created a draft booking identifier in the URL (/bookings/{uuid}/payment). No hold timer was shown, and whether the draft holds inventory is not verified [R243]. These are the only two write-like effects recorded on any engine.

You can do this through your account in the \"My Trips\" section." | [R144] [R142] | Officially documented. Area not observed |

Not verified: whether a booking made on RSG's engine for an operator resort carries an operator confirmation number and can be retrieved or changed with the operator tools. This requires a real booking in an authorised environment. The FAQ for R1 says the guest "will receive a confirmation on-screen once the payment is completed, along with an email and SMS confirming your booking." [R144]. That statement is documentation. The confirmation screen, email and SMS were not observed.

### 9.8 Official distribution or developer documentation found

Two official sources were found and both were read through a summarising fetch tool: Accor's developer portal, which says "Sign up easily to start using for free our APIs" and names no specific booking API on its landing page [R253], and Sabre's SynXis Channel Connect page (version 10.40 shown), which describes an interface that "integrates SynXis CR directly to the third-party travel sites" with "Real time availability of ARI (availability, rates and inventory)" [R254]. No official public API documentation was located for Marriott, IHG, Hyatt, Four Seasons, Ennismore or Jayasom in the time available, and absence of a find is not proof of absence. No endpoint was probed on any operator. Not verified: how RSG's own engine is connected to operators (channel manager, direct connect or manual allotment).

## 10. Field-level booking and registration data dictionary

Evidence class for this section: directly observed field labels on rendered forms, unless the "Journey step" column says the row is a UI text string read from page source. Nothing was typed into any field, so "Validation observed" records only what appeared without input. "None" or "Not triggered" in that column means no validation was provoked. It does not mean the field has no validation.

The "Journey step" column starts with the form type, so that corporate inquiry, newsletter subscription, vendor registration, residential sales inquiry, account creation and sign-in, booking inquiry, actual reservation, arrival registration and reservation lookup stay separate. Route codes (R1, R1b, R2) are defined in Section 9.2. Loyalty enrollment has no form on any RSG site. It appears only as a pre-ticked checkbox on Marriott and as membership prompts on IHG, Six Senses, Accor and SLS (Section 8.2). No online guest registration or check-in form was found on any RSG site [R095] [R113].

Three points matter when reading the table. First, on the destination-site engine the rendered step 4 form has no nationality, date of birth, ID, passport or address field [R223], while UI text strings embedded in the same site's page source name those fields and a passport-expiry validation message [R258]. Which of them are mandatory, at which step and for which guest is not verified, and requires a walked booking or a field specification from RSG. Second, on the RSG Hospitality engine the form asks for no identity document, but the Shebara transfer add-on asks guests to email "A copy of passports and visa or ID" and flight details to the resort concierge [R232]. Third, no operator engine asked for date of birth, passport or national ID at booking, and nationality was asked only by Accor for Raffles [R182].
