Hidden fields recorded on the RSG Hospitality guest form include browser_screen_height, browser_time, browser_useragent, browser_language and creditCardType [R230]. Inference in the notes: they look like inputs to card authentication. That is not confirmed. Not verified: fields at step 05 of the destination-site flow, child guest fields at step 4 when children are in the party, validation rules for names, email and phone on any form, and the Hyatt form.

## 11. Pricing, payment, policy and arrival matrices

### 11.1 How to read these matrices

Every price row is an observation for one tested scenario at one moment on 12 October 2026. It is labelled as such in the table and is not a quote. Rows marked "API output" were obtained by replaying the destination site's own search request and were not seen as rendered screens. Prices on different channels are not comparable without adjustment: some engines show a pre-tax nightly average, some show tax-inclusive stay totals, and the Marriott total for the St. Regis includes a transfer charge. No policy, price or behaviour in this section should be generalised from one resort, rate plan or channel to another.

What the price observations establish, and no more than this. Where tax was itemised on an operator engine, the effective rate was 20.75 percent, labelled either as 15.75% VAT plus 5% municipality tax or as 15% VAT, 5% municipality tax and a further 5% of VAT [R240] [R244]. The destination-site engine's breakdown showed a single "VAT" line of 15% and no municipality line [R219]. The Marriott "with taxes and fees" figure for the St. Regis includes a 2,382.80 SAR "Transfer / Transportation Charge" [R239]. Member prices were lower than public prices on Marriott, IHG, Accor and SLS in the scenarios observed [R239] [R240] [R182] [R243]. No per-child or extra-bed pricing was itemised on any operator engine. Not verified: any Hyatt price, and whether the "Red Sea Residents Escape" rate on Six Senses requires proof of residency.

The published booking terms for the destination-site channel add channel-level rules. Cancellation policy "varies" by hotel and is displayed in the reservation form, some rates are non-refundable, no-shows are not refunded, date changes are made by logging in and using a "Change my dates" form, and reservations not amended through RETC are subject to a 100% charge [R168] [R168]. A text string in the booking flow says changes or cancellations must be processed "at least 96 hours prior to check-in" [R258]. Which rule governs a given booking is not verified and requires RSG confirmation. Minimum-stay rules were seen only for Jayasom ("MIN. 1 NIGHT" on one retreat, longer programmes per the operator) and for opening offers [R248] [R118]. No engine returned a minimum-stay refusal for the test dates.

General rules published for Red Sea International Airport transfers: "All transfers must be pre-booked at least 48 hours before your arrival", bookings under 24 hours "are subject to waiting time", and each guest is allowed "one large suitcase, one small suitcase, and one standard laptop bag or handbag" [R260]. Seaplane conditions: "all Fly Red Sea flight bookings must be made at least seven days in advance", guests "must be able to enter and exit the aircraft without assistance", passengers over 32 weeks pregnant "will not be accepted", check-in needs a photo ID, and a marine transfer is offered at no extra cost if a flight is cancelled [R260] [R267]. A guest who arrives without a booking cannot book a seaplane at that time [R267]. The complimentary shuttle in the booking terms names only Edition, IHG, SLS, Turtle Bay Beach Club and Six Senses Southern Dunes [R168]. Wheelchairs and child seats on transfers carry "No additional charges, pre-booking is required" [R260].

Not verified: transfer price lists, boat and shuttle timetables, seaplane operating hours, passenger weight limits, the legal operator of land and marine transfers, AMAALA transfer lead times and prices, and whether Fly Red Sea operates to AMAALA today. These require RSG mobility, Fly Red Sea or customer care.

### 11.6 Entry requirements that depend on the traveller (Saudi government sources)

There is no universal visa rule. The table reproduces the dependency structure recorded from Visit Saudi (page data "Updated on 28 Jun 2026") and the official eVisa portal. It is time-sensitive, and the two government sources disagree on the eligible-country list (Section 15). No government or RSG source publishes a special visa arrangement for The Red Sea or AMAALA, and visa-on-arrival facilities at RSI are not verified [R275] [R276] [R282].

| **Depends on** | **Case** | **Route(s)** | **Fee shown** | **Insurance** | **Passport validity** | **Minors** | **Notes** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Nationality | GCC national (Bahrain, Kuwait, Oman, Qatar, UAE) | No visa | None | Not stated | "ID or Passport" | Not stated | None |
| Nationality | Russia | Visa exemption | None | None | "valid passport" | "may be subject to additional requirements, including official parental or guardian consent" | Up to 90 days within a calendar year |
| Nationality | 65 "eligible" nationalities (67 list entries) | eVisa via visa.visitsaudi.com; visa on arrival (kiosk or passport control); transit visa | 395 (about USD 105.32) | Included | 6 months beyond entry; US citizens only need a valid passport | "at any age"; under 18 without guardian check home-country rules | Multiple entry 1 year / 90 consecutive days, or single entry 3 months |
| Nationality | Ireland | eVisa; visa on arrival; Visa Waiver; transit | 395; waiver SAR 150 (about USD 40) | Included for eVisa and visa on arrival | 6 months | At least 18 to enter alone; under 18 with parent, grandparent or adult sibling | Waiver: single entry, max 180 days, apply 90 days to 48 hours before travel, from outside the Kingdom |
| Nationality | United Kingdom | eVisa; visa on arrival; Electronic Travel Authorization; transit | 395; ETA 50 (about USD 13.5) non-refundable | Included for eVisa and visa on arrival | 6 months | Not stated for ETA | ETA: multiple entry, valid 730 days, stay 180 days, 1 to 3 business days |
| Nationality + existing visa or residency | "noneligible" nationality (160 entries) holding a used US, UK or Schengen tourist or business visa, or US, UK or EU permanent residence | eVisa via ksavisa.sa; visa on arrival at passport control; transit | eVisa 339.44 (about USD 90.50); visa on arrival 395 | eVisa: not included. Visa on arrival: included | 6 months | Not stated | Qualifying visa must have a physical entry stamp from the issuing country; digital stamps not accepted |
| Nationality + residency | "noneligible" nationality resident in a GCC country | eVisa via ksavisa.sa; transit | 339.44 | Not included | 6 months; GCC residency valid 3 months beyond entry | Parent must apply first | No visa on arrival listed. First-degree family can be added |
| Nationality | "noneligible", none of the above | Embassy, consulate or Tasheer; transit | Not shown | Not included | 6 months | At least 18 to enter alone | Documents: residence proof, return ticket, employment proof, bank statement, itinerary, ID, address in Saudi |
| Nationality | Group D (Chad, Ethiopia, Iran, Libya, Palestine) | Embassy, consulate or Tasheer only | Not shown | Not included | 6 months | At least 18 to enter alone | No eVisa, visa on arrival or transit option listed |
| Nationality + purchase channel | "Visa by Package" nationalities (Bangladesh, Egypt, India, Indonesia, Jordan, Mexico, Pakistan) with no qualifying visa or GCC residency | Package visa via qualified travel agencies; embassy or Tasheer; transit | 402.21 including fees | Included | Not stated for package | Up to 5 children under 18 may accompany | Needs 4-star-plus licensed hotel and round-trip flight; min value 4,000 per adult for two days; 2 to 88 days; travel starts 48 h or more after purchase |
| Passport type | Diplomatic or special passport, any nationality | Embassy only | Not shown | Not stated | Not stated | Not stated | Not eligible for online application |
| Routing | Passing through with a Saudia or flynas ticket | Transit or stopover visa | Free; application about SAR 39.50; insurance from about SAR 13 | Charged separately | Not stated | Not stated | Single entry, stay up to 96 hours |
| Travel date | Hajj season | Makkah restrictions for visit-visa holders | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | Only a 2025 notice is published [R282]; 2026 and 2027 dates not verified |
| Entry point | RSI or Al Wajh | Not addressed by any source | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | eVisa "valid for entry through all airports and / or seaports" [R282]; visa-on-arrival facilities at RSI not verified |

National rules recorded from government sources, for reference only: Visit Saudi's FAQ states "The consumption of alcohol is illegal in Saudi Arabia.", "Unmarried couples are able to share accommodation." and "Tourists are not permitted to fly drones in the Kingdom." [R277]. ZATCA requires disclosure of cash above SAR 40,000 (page updated 26 Aug 2026) [R284]. SFDA runs a "Traveler's Permit for Controlled Drugs" service [R287]. Neither destination site publishes its own position on alcohol, unmarried couples, Ramadan, photography or drones, and resort beverage copy refers to non-alcoholic drinks without stating a policy [R098] [R106]. Not verified: Ramadan rules, health and vaccination entry rules, and the national public decency regulation text (the official link returned 404) [R277].

For bookings made on visitredsea.com or amaala.com, the booking terms route changes and cancellations to RETC, and say that in-stay extras and alternate accommodation are matters for the hotel [R168]. Seaplane disruption is handled by Fly Red Sea, and airline baggage problems by the airline [R260] [R268]. For a booking made directly with an operator, the operator's own lookup and contact channels apply (Section 9.7). Not verified: live chat hours and languages, any guest WhatsApp channel, call-centre languages, an in-destination emergency contact, the complaint escalation path and refund timelines.

## 12. Privacy and public technical findings

Evidence class for this section: officially documented legal pages (quoted only in fragments of under 15 words, otherwise closely paraphrased), fetched HTML, live observation of page source and loaded hosts, and a small number of third-party vendor sources. No compliance conclusion is drawn anywhere in this section. Every legal statement must be re-read at source before legal use.

### 12.1 Entities and documents

There are four distinct RSG-family document sets, and three legal entities appear depending on the document. The destination privacy notices (visitredsea.com and amaala.com) name "Red Sea Global and its subsidiaries" as the "Data Controller" [R291] [R113]. The Booking General Terms & Conditions on visitredsea.com define the contracting party as Regenerative Escapes Tourism Company (RETC), a limited liability company with commercial registration number 1010916245, describe hotels as "RETC suppliers" that are independent contractors, and state that the site is "powered by Regenerative Escapes Tourism Company" [R168]. The Turtle Bay Hotel privacy policy names Red Sea Global Hospitality as operator and data controller [R294]. None of the documents shows a last-updated date, names a data protection officer, or mentions a chatbot or AI assistant [R291] [R293].

What the destination privacy notice discloses, in paraphrase: four data categories (account profile data, behavioural data, booking data and travel documentation data, the last including National ID, Iqama, passport or visa details "where required"); purposes that include building profiles and "predictive models through the use of AI or algorithms"; sharing with vendors, advisors, authorities and "RSG affiliates", with hotel operators not named as a recipient category; storage and processing in Saudi Arabia with international transfer "where permitted by the PDPL"; a right not to be subject to a decision "based solely on automated processing, including profiling"; and requests fulfilled "within 30 calendar days" through the Privacy Center, which is a web form hosted on rsg-privacy.my.onetrust.com [R291] [R136]. The amaala.com notice is the same text and defines "the Website" as www.visitredsea.com [R113]. The Shebara reservations engine embeds a privacy block whose scope is stated as www.redseaglobal.com and which does not mention reservations or payment data [R122].

Inference in the notes: a guest who books on visitredsea.com appears to deal with three named parties (RETC as contracting party and merchant, "Red Sea Global and its subsidiaries" as controller, and the hotel as supplier), and the documents do not explain how those roles relate for personal data. Not verified: the contracting entity and merchant of record for bookings on the RSG Hospitality engine, whether operators receive guest personal data and on what basis, and clause-by-clause equivalence of the Arabic versions.

Use is "strictly for personal and non-commercial purposes only". The user will not use the site to provide RETC bookings, pricing or availability to other persons or organisations. RETC may refuse "suspicious bookings" and may "blacklist" users | Destination-site booking channel | [R168] |

No right to use "any trademark, logo" without the owner's prior written permission. No named clause on robots, scraping, crawling or framing (checked by keyword) | All three sites | [R003] [R104] [R113] |

robots.txt files allow general crawling on the three main sites and disallow /web/guest/, /*?utm and /*/url-builder on visitredsea.com and amaala.com. The Shebara reservations engine disallows everything except search and offer pages. No robots file names an AI crawler [R292] [R122]. A robots file is not a permission to use booking data. Inference in the notes: the booking terms' personal-use and no-redistribution wording is the clause that an AI booking agent operating against visitredsea.com without RSG's agreement would most directly meet, and whether a guest-authorised agent counts as the guest's "personal" use is a legal question the notes do not answer. Keyword searches can miss clauses phrased differently.

### 12.4 Consent tool and loaded third-party hosts

visitredsea.com, amaala.com and the RSG Hospitality resort sites load OneTrust. The configuration served for the research location (reported as Egypt) is opt-in, with banner buttons "Accept All Cookies" and "Cookies Settings" and no reject-all button. Categories are Strictly Necessary, Targeting, Performance, Functional and Social Media, with all but the first inactive by default [R095] [R113]. The first-visit banner was not seen rendered. With consent stored as strictly necessary only, hosts that still loaded on visitredsea.com included googletagmanager.com, google-analytics.com, browser-intake-datadoghq.eu, two googlesyndication hosts, reCAPTCHA Enterprise and attachuk.imi.chat [R095]. The booking workstream separately recorded requests to browser-intake-datadoghq.eu and google-analytics.com after optional categories were declined [R219]. On redseaglobal.com no OneTrust script or banner was present in one session on the shared profile, while advertising and analytics hosts loaded [R138]. No cookie banner was shown on the RSG Hospitality reservations engine, where advertising cookies were present [R227]. These are observations from single sessions. Not verified: banner behaviour for a visitor in Saudi Arabia or the EU, and behaviour on a clean first visit.

### 12.5 Saudi data protection framework (official guidance only)

SDAIA's "Guide to the Saudi Personal Data Protection Law, For Controllers and Processors" (December 2023, Version 1.0) names the Personal Data Protection Law, approved by Royal Decree No. 19/m dated 1443/2/9, says it "came into force on 14 September 2023" with a one-year grace period, names SDAIA as the Competent Authority, and lists a "Regulation on Personal Data Transfer outside the Kingdom" [R300]. The guide states that it is guidance and contains no binding rules. RSG's destination privacy notice refers to "PDPL" and directs unresolved complaints to SDAIA [R291]. Not verified: the law text itself, the amending decree, the transfer regulation, and whether SDAIA remains the Competent Authority after the two-year period described in the guide.

Additional identifiers recorded: the destination-site engine uses hotel IDs such as Shebara 1035 and exposes a same-origin API under /api/booking/v1 and /v3 [R225]. Script bundles on amaala.com reference an API host api.pro.webconnectorprojects.com [R151]. Browser telemetry on the booking pages is tagged "service:cvp-web-app, env:prod" [R225]. Live chat assets load from /chat/js/imichatwidgetv2.js with session keys named webex_engage_data-org [R095]. The payment provider is not visible in any page read.

### 12.7 Public statements about apps, AI and technology partners

RSG's own channels confirm an official Visit Red Sea app with booking, "My Trips" and "24/7 concierge service" [R095] [R304]. No RSG-issued press release announcing an AI assistant or chatbot was found [R138] [R023]. Three vendor sources exist and were read through a summarising fetch tool, so they are third-party and carry paraphrase risk. An Adobe release (9 October 2025) says the Visit Red Sea properties are built on Adobe Experience Manager with Globant leading implementation [R301]. A Globant release (12 February 2025) describes a "Connected Visitor Experience" partnership and quotes RSG's Group Head of Technology and Corporate Excellence [R302]. A Globant blog (21 September 2026) says RSG, TOURISE and AWS launched an "Agentic Tourism Corridor" in which agents for the Red Sea and AlUla check availability and assemble a journey. It does not say the agents complete bookings, and no specification, documentation or access terms were found [R303]. This last item is uncorroborated by any official RSG, TOURISE or AWS source and must not be treated as an established RSG capability. RSG's own releases mention an stc agreement that includes "AI-driven services" and an AI venture fund with Bunat Ventures [R050] [R056]. Inference in the notes: publicly, "concierge" and "chat" are described as support services and AI is described as personalisation and profiling.

### 12.8 Technical discovery questions for RSG or operators

These questions are reproduced from the privacy and technical notes. They are questions, not design decisions.

**Integration methods**
- Which entity (RSG, RETC, RSGH) owns the booking capability on visitredsea.com and amaala.com, and who can authorise a third-party agent to use it?
- Is there any supported programmatic interface for search, booking and servicing, or is the website and app the only channel?
- What is the "Agentic Tourism Corridor" protocol referred to by Globant, who governs it, and can an external agent participate?
- How do third-party operator resorts (Marriott, IHG, Hyatt, Accor, Four Seasons, Rosewood, Equinox and others) receive bookings made on visitredsea.com, and do operator terms permit an AI agent to place them?
- Is the reservations.<brand>.sa engine a separate inventory source from visitredsea.com for Shebara, Desert Rock and Turtle Bay?
- Does the booking terms' personal-use and no-redistribution clause apply to a guest-authorised agent, and will RSG give written permission for the POC?

**Sandbox availability**
- Is there a non-production environment with test inventory, test rates and test payment?
- Can test bookings be created and cancelled without reaching hotels or charging cards?
- Are test accounts and test guest profiles available, and who issues them?

**Authentication and authorization**
- How would an agent authenticate: guest account login, a partner credential, or delegated guest consent?
- Is guest login required to book, and what identity checks (OTP, email verification, reCAPTCHA) sit in the flow?
- What scopes or roles would limit an agent to search, hold, book or modify?
- How would RSG distinguish an authorised agent from the automated traffic that Cloudflare and reCAPTCHA currently challenge?

**Reservation creation and confirmation**
- What is the authoritative confirmation event: payment capture, confirmation email with voucher, or a status in "My Trips"?
- How are "request" bookings, which need Customer Support confirmation, represented and how long do they take?
- What guest data is mandatory at booking (travel document details, flight numbers, accompanying guest names)?
- Can one transaction include rooms, experiences, mobility and dining, and are those confirmed separately?

**Inventory and rate access**
- What is the source of truth for availability and rates per resort, and how fresh is what the site shows?
- Which rate types exist (refundable, non-refundable, member, promo-code, packages) and are any restricted from agent sale?
- How are taxes (municipality tax and VAT) and currency conversion presented and guaranteed?
- What are the booking window limits (the FAQ states at least one day ahead and up to 6 months)?

**Cancellation and modification**
- Which cancellation policy governs a given booking: the hotel's, RETC's or the 96-hour rule shown in the booking flow text?
- Can modifications be made programmatically, or only through the "Change my dates" and "Send a message" forms?
- What fees apply and how are refunds issued and timed?

**Duplicate-booking prevention**
- Is there an idempotency key or cart identifier that prevents a retried request from creating a second booking?
- How does the system treat two near-identical bookings for the same guest and dates?
- What triggers the "suspicious bookings" refusal and blacklisting described in the booking terms?

**Payment handling**
- Who is merchant of record (the terms name RETC and "RETC Payment Services")?
- Which payment provider and methods are live (terms list Mada, Visa, Mastercard, American Express, bank transfer; the FAQ adds Apple Pay)?
- Can payment be completed by the guest on an RSG-hosted page so the agent never handles card data?
- Is 3-D Secure or another step-up required, and how is it surfaced?

**Session recovery**
- How long does a cart or held selection persist, and can it be resumed on another device or channel?
- If payment fails or the session drops after payment, how is booking state reconciled?
- Is there a reference a guest or agent can use to resume or query an incomplete booking?

**Human handoff**
- Which platform runs the "live assistant chat", and is it staffed, automated or both?
- Can an external agent hand a conversation to RSG's chat or call centre with context attached?
- What are the staffed hours and languages, and what are the routes for same-day, multi-resort and large-group requests that the FAQ sends to chat?

**Audit and data handling**
- Which entity is controller for booking data when RETC contracts, RSG is named controller and a hotel is supplier?
- Which personal data is passed to hotel operators and under what terms?
- Where is booking and chat data stored, and which international transfers occur?
- What logs of agent actions would RSG require, and for how long?
- What retention applies to chat transcripts and booking records?
- Is there a DPO or named privacy contact for the POC, beyond the Privacy Center form and Privacy@RedSeaGlobal.com?
- What consent wording would RSG require before an agent submits a guest's passport, ID or flight details?

## 13. Evidence-linked POC readiness assessment

This section assesses the evidence behind each capability that a possible AI agent proof of concept might include. It is not a design, and it reaches no finding that any capability is feasible or ready. The existence of a public interface is recorded as evidence that a function exists for human customers. It is not treated as evidence that an agent may use it, because the published terms restrict use and no supported access route is documented (Section 12.3). The agent described here is a concept under evaluation. Nothing in the evidence shows that RSG operates such an agent today.

| **Proposed capability** | **Supporting evidence** | **Required information** | **External dependency** | **Permission/access needed** | **What remains unknown** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Answering destination and resort questions | Public content exists per resort on visitredsea.com and amaala.com (rooms, occupancy, dining, access, policies) [R099] [R148], and RSG publishes corporate facts [R001]. The evidence also shows that this content is inconsistent: Section 15 registers the conflicts found, English and Arabic pages state different facts [R014] [R116], and corporate pages are stale [R014] | An owner-confirmed content source per property with an "as of" date, an approved English and Arabic name list, room-type master data, and a status per property (announced, reservable, open) | RSG content owners, RSG Hospitality, and each third-party operator for its own property facts | Written licence to use site copy, images, logos and trademarks. Site terms allow "personal and non-commercial use" only [R104] [R003] | Which source RSG treats as authoritative for each fact. Whether operators permit their property content to be restated. Tone-of-voice and brand rules for English and Arabic |
| Helping compare verified options | No comparison tool exists on any RSG site [R098]. Resort pages show no prices [R099]. Comparable attributes are published per resort but in different depth, for example accessibility and child policy [R158] [R164] [R266] | A normalised attribute set per resort (location type, age policy, meal basis, access mode, accessibility, transfer time) with a source and date for each value, and the rule for handling attributes that are not published | RSG content owners and operators. Live rates for any price comparison | Same content licence as above. RSG agreement on how options may be ranked or recommended across RSG-operated and third-party resorts | Whether RSG wants neutral comparison or guided recommendation. How to treat resorts that are listed but not bookable (Raffles, Grand Hyatt) [R098] |
| Collecting travel preferences | The existing search inputs are resort, dates, nights, adults, children and child ages [R095]. A three-question preference form exists ("REQUEST A QUOTE") but rendered blank options on the one page tested [R140]. No audience or persona statement was found [R027] | The preference questions RSG wants asked, the allowed answer values, and the consent wording for storing them. Each child's exact age, because age bands differ by resort and service [R258] [R130] | RSG marketing and customer-care teams. RSG privacy function | Agreement on what preference data may be collected and retained. The privacy notice lists profiling and "predictive models through the use of AI or algorithms" as purposes [R291] | Whether collected preferences may be written to any RSG system. Retention period for conversation data. The intended audience segments |
| Searching current availability and rates | A same-origin search interface exists behind the destination sites and returned rates in the tested scenarios [R225] [R219]. The same resorts returned different availability on two RSG-run channels for the same dates [R220] [R228]. Operator engines returned their own rates [R239] [R240]. The booking terms prohibit gathering or extracting "information relating to hotel pricing and availability", including by "'screen scraping' software" [R168] | An authorised, documented source of availability and rates per resort, the rate types that may be offered, the tax basis of each price, and the booking window rules | RETC and RSG digital teams for the destination-site engine. RSG Hospitality for the brand-site engine. Each operator for its own inventory | Written permission and a supported access method. Public access is not a permission: the terms restrict use to "personal and non-commercial purposes" [R168], and Cloudflare and reCAPTCHA challenge automated traffic [R095] | Whether any programmatic interface, sandbox or partner access exists. Which channel is the source of truth when two RSG channels disagree. Why the destination-site engine returned no 2027 rates for RSG-operated hotels |
| Collecting required booking details | The fields on each reachable guest form are recorded in Section 10. The destination-site form asks for title, names, email, mobile and optional requests [R223]. UI text strings also name nationality, date of birth, passport, visa and ID fields that were not seen on the rendered form [R258]. Operator forms differ, and Accor alone asks for nationality at booking [R182] | The definitive list of mandatory fields per channel and per step, including any travel-document fields, flight details and transfer details. Validation rules for each field | RETC and RSG digital teams. Operators for their own forms | Consent wording approved by RSG before an agent collects passport, ID or flight details. A walked booking in an authorised test environment | Which identity fields are mandatory, at which step, and for which guest. Step 05 of the destination-site flow was not reached. No validation message was triggered on any form |
| Creating an account if actually needed | No route required an account to reach the guest-details page [R223] [R239]. Registration on visitredsea.com starts with one email field and the hint "Use the email from your booking" [R095]. Post-booking changes are described as an account feature ("My Trips") [R144] | Whether an account is mandatory at any point, what registration asks for after step 1, and whether one account covers visitredsea.com, amaala.com, the app and the RSG Hospitality engine | RSG digital and identity teams | RSG decision on whether a third party may create accounts on a guest's behalf. A test account | Everything after the first Register step. The identity service behind the drawer. Whether a guest-checkout booking appears in "My Trips" |
| Managing verification steps | The destination-site guest form label reads "E-Mail (We'll send a verification code) *" [R223]. A reCAPTCHA response field is present on the guest form [R142]. Marriott showed a timed hold on the guest page [R239]. No one-time code, CAPTCHA challenge or 3-D Secure step was observed anywhere, because nothing was submitted | The complete list of verification steps in each flow (email code, SMS, CAPTCHA, card authentication), who must complete each one, and time limits | RETC, the payment provider, and each operator | Verification codes and CAPTCHAs are completed by the customer. An agent role in them would need explicit RSG and provider approval | Whether the email code is mandatory for guest checkout and when it is sent. Whether SMS verification exists. How RSG would distinguish an authorised agent from traffic that bot protection challenges |
| Reviewing choices with the customer | The destination-site flow shows a Booking Summary with a tax-inclusive total and a "Price Breakdown" dialog [R219]. Rate cancellation dialogs exist per rate plan, and the transfer cancellation dialog returned an error in the session [R221]. Policies differ by channel and rate (Section 11) | For the selected rate: total price and tax basis, inclusions, cancellation and deposit terms, transfer charges, and the entity the customer is contracting with | RETC for destination-site bookings, RSG Hospitality or the operator for other channels | Authority to present terms on RSG's behalf, and the approved wording for doing so | The review and terms-acceptance screen at step 05 was not observed. Which cancellation rule governs a booking: the hotel's, RETC's, or the 96-hour rule in the booking-flow text [R258] [R168] |
| Handling payment through an approved process | Published statements exist: the booking terms list Mada, Visa, Mastercard, American Express and bank transfer, and name "RETC Payment Services" [R168]. The FAQ adds Apple Pay [R144]. No payment screen was observed on the destination-site flow. Operator and brand-site engines show card fields on the guest page [R230] [R243] | The approved payment method for the POC, the merchant of record, the payment provider, and whether the customer can complete payment on an RSG-hosted page | RETC, its payment provider, and RSG finance | An approved process in which the customer enters payment details directly. An agent handling card data is outside what any observed evidence supports | The payment provider, the payment page, card authentication steps, deposit and prepayment timing on the destination-site channel, and refund handling |
| Submitting a reservation | No reservation was submitted on any channel. On the destination-site flow no booking, cart or hold request was sent to the server up to step 4 [R223]. On Marriott a rate click started a timed hold, and on SLS a rate click created a draft booking identifier [R239] [R243]. The booking terms say RETC may refuse "suspicious bookings" and may "blacklist" users [R168] | The supported way to create a reservation, what creates a hold, how duplicates are prevented, and what data the submit step requires | RETC, RSG Hospitality, and each operator. Operator site terms for any operator channel: Marriott and Equinox prohibit automated access, and IHG limits reservation facilities to bookings for a person the user is "legally authorized to act" for [R295] [R299] [R296] | Written authorisation from the entity that owns each booking channel, and a test environment or an agreed real booking with agreed cancellation | Everything after the final submit button on every channel. Whether a server-side hold exists on the destination-site flow. Whether RSG-channel bookings reach operator systems with an operator confirmation number |
| Establishing that a booking is confirmed | Documentation only. The booking terms say a booking is confirmed and final only after payment is received and RETC emails a confirmation with a hotel voucher, and that "request" bookings are confirmed only by email from Customer Support [R168]. The FAQ says confirmation appears on screen and by email and SMS [R144]. Routes /booking/checkout/success and /booking/checkout/failure exist as strings only [R099] | The authoritative confirmation event and identifier, how a failed or interrupted payment is reconciled, and how a "request" booking is represented | RETC and its payment provider | Access to the confirmation status through an RSG-approved channel | No confirmation screen, email, SMS or voucher was observed. The format of the booking reference is unknown |
| Providing confirmation information | Documentation only, as above [R168] [R144]. Arrival obligations are published: all RSI transfers pre-booked at least 48 hours ahead and seaplanes at least seven days ahead [R260] | A sample confirmation email, voucher and SMS, the pre-arrival instructions per resort, and the sender brand for each channel | RETC, RSG Hospitality, and operators | RSG-supplied samples and approval of what an agent may restate | Brand and sender of confirmation messages on each path. Whether transfer, luggage and car-entry-permit steps are part of confirmation |
| Handing off exceptions to a person | RSG already routes several cases to people: bookings under 24 hours and multi-resort bookings go to "the live assistant chat" [R144], yacht, private-jet and own-car arrivals are followed up by staff [R221], and Thuwal Private Retreat is enquiry only [R127]. A chat widget loads on both destination sites [R095]. One phone number and several email addresses are published [R110] [R260] | The list of cases that must go to a person, the receiving team for each, staffed hours and languages, and what context may be passed | RSG contact centre (RETC), resort reservations desks, and operators for bookings made on operator channels | Agreement on a handoff route into RSG's chat or call centre, and on the data that may accompany it | Whether the chat is staffed, automated or both. Published hours conflict ("Daily 8:00 am - 12:00 pm" against "24/7") [R110] [R105]. Complaint escalation path and refund timelines |

Three patterns run through the table. Every capability from "searching current availability and rates" onward depends on permission and access that only the channel owner can give. Every capability that touches the customer's identity, payment or verification was not observed past the guest-details page, so the required information is partly unknown. And every capability that restates RSG or operator content depends on an authoritative source that does not yet exist publicly in a consistent form.

## 14. Knowledge-base content inventory

This inventory classifies the information found, by how it would need to be handled if it were ever used to answer customers. It is an inventory of evidence, not a content design. Five categories are used: stable information, dynamic information needing a live authoritative source, property-specific policies, information needing customer context, and questions that must be escalated. "Stable" means slow-changing. It does not mean verified as correct, and several stable items carry conflicts that RSG would need to settle first.

| **Category** | **Topic** | **What the evidence provides** | **Authoritative source to use** | **Handling note** | **Source** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Stable information | Corporate identity and ownership | Name, former name, PIF ownership wording, board chair, vision, mission and values in exact wording | RSG About, Leadership and legal pages | Quote RSG's own wording. No "purpose" statement exists. Addresses conflict between documents | [R001] [R007] [R004] |
| 1. Stable information | Dated milestones | Timeline from 31 Jul 2017 to 1 Oct 2026 with the status wording of each release | RSG press releases | Attribute to RSG. Older releases were retitled, and several dates conflict (Section 15) | [R035] [R083] |
| 1. Stable information | Brand architecture and naming | Brand layers, names as written in English and Arabic, taglines | Official sites | No approved glossary exists publicly. Arabic spellings vary. Needs RSG's approved name list before use | [R002] [R095] [R113] |
| 1. Stable information | Resort identity and location | Official names, island or area, operator brand, published key counts | visitredsea.com, amaala.com, operator sites | Several key counts conflict. Keep "as of" dates | [R098] [R191] |
| 1. Stable information | Room and villa categories | Category names, sizes and published occupancy per resort | Resort pages on the destination sites and operator sites | Load from owner-confirmed data, never generate. Desert Rock lists differ between two official sites. Data errors exist on some cards | [R158] [R100] [R123] |
| 1. Stable information | Physical accessibility features per resort | Accessible room counts and features where published | Resort pages | Depth varies by resort and several resorts publish nothing. Do not infer | [R158] [R100] [R266] |
| 1. Stable information | Activity conditions | Minimum ages, waiver, medical certificate for Galaxea diving, life jackets | visitredsea.com and amaala.com experience pages | Stated per product. No weight or height limits are published | [R140] [R256] [R117] |
| 1. Stable information | Seaplane conditions | Unaided boarding, pregnancy limits, photo ID, fare bands by age, baggage | RSI airport site and flyredsea.com | Baggage allowance conflicts between the two sources | [R260] [R130] |
| 1. Stable information | Sustainability targets and certifications | Targets, reported results and certifications with dates | RSG pages and releases | Cite one dated source per number. RSG's own figures conflict | [R016] [R067] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Availability and rates | Single observations for tested scenarios only (Section 11) | The booking system of the channel on which the customer will book | Never restate an observed price as current. Two RSG channels disagreed on availability for the same dates | [R225] [R228] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Opening status of each property | Status groups as of 12 Oct 2026 (Section 6) | RSG and operator announcements, and the live booking channel | Inventory changed monthly in 2026. "Opening" announcements preceded first guests by months | [R083] [R170] [R113] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Flight routes and schedules | Widget snapshot on 12 Oct 2026 | Airlines | Three statements on one site disagree. Direct the customer to the airline | [R106] [R167] [R199] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Offers and promotions | Four listed promotions and AMAALA opening offers | Live offers pages and the booking engine | An expired offer was still promoted on the access date | [R141] [R118] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Operational notices | "Surf Lagoon is temporarily closed." | Live site or RSG operations | Time-sensitive | [R095] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Transfer options and prices | Observed transfer prices for tested scenarios, and published lead-time rules | Booking flow or customer care | No source publishes timetables or a price list | [R221] [R260] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Visa rules and fees | Visit Saudi groups and fees, "Updated on 28 Jun 2026" | Visit Saudi and the official eVisa portal | Government sources disagree on the eligible-country list. Always send the customer to the official portal | [R275] [R281] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Customs thresholds | ZATCA page updated 26 Aug 2026 | ZATCA | The RSI airport site quotes a different threshold | [R284] [R264] |
| 2. Dynamic information needing a live authoritative source | Support hours and channels | Published contacts and conflicting hours | RSG contact centre | Hours conflict and chat hours are not published | [R110] [R105] |
| 3. Property-specific policies | Cancellation and deposit | Per property, per channel and per rate plan (Section 11.3) | The policy shown for the selected rate on the booking channel | Never generalise. The same property shows different windows on different channels | [R158] [R122] [R245] |
| 3. Property-specific policies | Children, extra beds and age limits | Surcharges and age bands per resort. Miraval is adults only. Equinox AMAALA is adults only per the operator | Resort policy text and operator | "Child" means different ages in different systems | [R162] [R214] [R098] |

| 4. Information needing customer context | Party composition | Each child's exact age changes room options, price, seaplane fare and activity eligibility | Customer | The destination-site widget silently dropped children when ages were blank | [R237] [R130] |
| 4. Information needing customer context | Arrival plan | Arrival airport, airline, flight number and time, own car, private jet or yacht | Customer | Lead time decides the options: seaplane needs seven days, transfers 48 hours, under 24 hours goes to chat | [R260] [R221] |
| 4. Information needing customer context | Accessibility needs | Mobility aid, transfer type, room features | Customer | The booking flow offers only a free-text request field. The seaplane requires unaided boarding | [R258] [R260] |
| 4. Information needing customer context | Booking channel used | Whether the customer booked on a destination site, an RSG Hospitality engine or an operator site | Customer | Decides who the counterparty is and where changes are made | [R168] [R249] |

| 5. Questions that must be escalated | Alcohol, unmarried couples, Ramadan, photography and drones at the destinations | National statements exist from Visit Saudi. Neither destination site publishes its own position | RSG policy owner | State the national rule with its source and escalate anything destination-specific. Do not imply a destination exception | [R277] |

## 15. Conflicting-source register

The register lists 128 conflicts recorded in the notes. Each row shows both positions with their sources. No conflict is resolved here by speculation. The "Status" column names the evidence that would settle it. The "Type" column shows whether the disagreement is between two official sources, between an official text and an observed interface, between language versions, or between an official and a third-party source.

## 18. Prioritized questions for our team and for RSG

P1 questions decide whether and how the work can proceed. P2 questions shape scope and content. P3 questions are useful but not blocking. The "Audience" column separates questions for our team, for RSG and for operators. The detailed technical discovery questions are in Section 12.8 and are not repeated here.

| **ID** | **Priority** | **Audience** | **Topic** | **Question** | **Why it matters** | **Evidence that prompted it** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q01 | P1 | Our team | Scope | Which booking channel is the POC meant to work with: the destination-site channel (RETC), the RSG Hospitality brand-site engine, operator channels, or none (advice and handoff only)? | Each channel has a different owner, different terms and different evidence gaps | Section 9.2 route overview |
| Q02 | P1 | Our team | Scope | Is the POC limited to RSG-operated resorts (Shebara, Desert Rock, Turtle Bay Hotel, Daraah, Thuwal), or does it include third-party operated resorts? | Operator resorts add operator terms and brand permissions | [R121] [R295] |
| Q03 | P1 | Our team | Permission | Who at our side will request written permission from RSG and RETC before any system touches booking data again? | The booking terms prohibit extraction of pricing and availability, and about 35 search requests were already replayed during research | [R168] [R225] |
| Q04 | P1 | Our team | Evidence | Who will repeat the key screens as a person in a normal browser and save screenshots with URL and time? | No screenshots exist. All evidence is textual | Section 2 |
| Q05 | P1 | Our team | Legal | Who will re-read the booking terms, site terms and privacy notices at source, in English and Arabic, before they are relied on? | Legal text was captured only in short fragments | [R168] [R291] |
| Q06 | P2 | Our team | Languages | Is Arabic in scope from the start? | Arabic copy is rewritten, not translated, and Arabic pages state different facts in places | [R079] [R014] |
| Q07 | P2 | Our team | Test data | Can we provide a test identity, mailbox, mobile number and device for an authorised walk of the remaining steps and the app? | Step 05, verification, the account area and the app were not reached | Section 17 |
| Q08 | P2 | Our team | Hyatt | Who will open the Hyatt pages manually for Miraval and Grand Hyatt? | Automated access was blocked and was not circumvented | [R242] |
| Q09 | P1 | RSG | Ownership and authority | Which entity (RSG, RETC, RSG Hospitality) owns the booking capability on visitredsea.com and amaala.com, and who can authorise a third party to work with it? | Three entities appear across the documents | [R168] [R291] [R027] |
| Q10 | P1 | RSG | Existing capability | Does RSG already operate, pilot or plan an AI assistant, chatbot or agent for guests, including the "Agentic Tourism Corridor" described by Globant? | Avoids duplicating or conflicting with an existing programme. Public official sources show none | [R303] [R023] |
| Q11 | P1 | RSG | Access | Is there any supported programmatic interface, partner programme or sandbox for search, booking and servicing? | None is publicly documented | [R292] |
| Q12 | P1 | RSG | Permission | Will RSG and RETC give written permission for the POC, given the personal-use and no-extraction clauses in the booking terms? | Public access is not a permission | [R168] |
| Q13 | P1 | RSG | Source of truth | Which channel is authoritative for availability and rates of RSG-operated resorts, and why did the destination-site engine return no 2027 rates while the brand-site engine did? | The two RSG-run channels disagreed for the same dates | [R220] [R228] |
| Q14 | P1 | RSG | Booking data | What data is mandatory to complete a booking, at which step and for which guest, including passport, visa, ID and flight details? | UI strings name identity fields that were not on the observed form | [R258] [R223] |
| Q15 | P1 | RSG | Confirmation | What is the authoritative confirmation event and reference, and how are "request" bookings confirmed? | Only documentation was read. No confirmation was observed | [R168] [R144] |
| Q16 | P1 | RSG | Payment | Who is merchant of record, which provider and methods are live, and can the customer complete payment on an RSG-hosted page? | The terms and the FAQ list different methods, and no payment screen was observed | [R168] [R144] |
| Q17 | P1 | RSG | Verification | What verification steps exist (email code, SMS, reCAPTCHA, card authentication), and how would RSG distinguish an authorised agent from traffic its bot protection challenges? | A verification-code label and reCAPTCHA field were seen | [R223] [R095] |
| Q18 | P1 | RSG | Handoff | Which platform runs the live chat, is it staffed or automated, what are the hours and languages, and can context be passed to it? | The site already routes several cases to chat, and published hours conflict | [R144] [R110] [R105] |
| Q19 | P1 | RSG | Inventory status | Can RSG supply an itemised list of resorts with status (announced, reservable, open), opening dates, operator and booking channel? | No single source itemises the 11 or the seven, and one hotel is unnamed | [R083] [R013] |
| Q20 | P1 | RSG | Policies | Which cancellation rule governs a destination-site booking: the hotel's, RETC's, or the 96-hour rule in the booking-flow text? | Channels and pages disagree | [R258] [R168] [R122] |
| Q21 | P2 | RSG | Accounts | Is an account required at any point, what does registration ask for, and is there one identity across visitredsea.com, amaala.com, the app and the RSG Hospitality engine? | Only the first registration screen was seen | [R095] [R230] |
| Q22 | P2 | RSG | Operators | How do third-party operator resorts receive destination-site bookings, do guests get an operator confirmation number, and can such bookings be changed on operator sites? | Not verified by any evidence | [R238] [R249] |
| Q23 | P2 | RSG | Taxes | Does the nett price on the destination-site engine already contain the 5% municipality fee, and what is the approved way to present taxes? | The two RSG-run channels present tax differently | [R219] [R230] |
| Q24 | P2 | RSG | Arrival | Can RSG supply transfer timetables, prices, lead-time rules per resort, the legal operator of transfers, and the status of seaplane service to AMAALA? | Not published, and sources conflict on AMAALA | [R260] [R200] [R130] |
| Q25 | P2 | RSG | AMAALA gateway | Which airport should be presented as the gateway for each AMAALA resort? | amaala.com and operator pages disagree | [R199] [R214] |
| Q26 | P2 | RSG | Brand | Which brand should speak at each stage on each path, and can RSG supply guidelines, approved names in English and Arabic, tone of voice and asset licences? | No public brand specification exists | [R023] Section 4.11 |
| Q27 | P2 | RSG | Content | Which source does RSG treat as authoritative for resort facts, and who owns corrections to the conflicts in Section 15? | Official pages disagree with each other | Section 15 |
| Q28 | P2 | RSG | Destination rules | What is the approved wording on alcohol, unmarried couples, Ramadan, photography and drones at each destination? | Only national statements exist | [R277] |
| Q29 | P2 | RSG | Privacy | Which entity is controller for booking data, what do operators receive, where are booking and chat data stored, and what consent wording is required before collecting passport or flight details? | The documents name three parties and no DPO | [R291] [R168] |
| Q30 | P2 | RSG | Daraah Resort | Is Daraah Resort open, where exactly is it, who operates it, and is it the "AMAALA Hotel" of the November 2025 release? | Status, location and name conflict | [R116] [R126] [R054] |
| Q31 | P3 | RSG | Loyalty | What is the planned benefits scheme behind "exclusive benefits will be ready soon", and when does it launch? | A banner references it and no scheme is published | [R219] |
| Q32 | P3 | RSG | Environments | Is RSG aware that production pages link to a UAT host that answers without authentication, and that test pages are in public sitemaps? | A security and brand exposure noted during research | [R156] [R137] |
| Q33 | P3 | RSG | Travel Trade | Can RSG share the Travel Trade toolkit files and their usage terms? | The download host did not resolve | [R103] |
| Q34 | P2 | Operators | Operator terms | What is each operator's position on an assistant placing or preparing bookings on its own site on a customer's behalf? | Marriott and Equinox prohibit automated access. IHG has an authorisation clause | [R295] [R299] [R296] |
| Q35 | P3 | Operators | Ennismore and Marriott | Does selecting a rate create an inventory hold or draft booking, and how long does it last? | Observed side effects on two engines | [R239] [R243] |
| Q36 | P3 | Operators | Fly Red Sea | What are the operating hours, minimum age, passenger weight rules and current routes, including AMAALA? | Not published or conflicting | [R130] [R260] |

## 19. Source and evidence register

This register merges the source registers of all nine notes files into one list of 308 sources with unified IDs. Sources with the same URL in different notes files were merged into one row. The "Claims supported" and "Limitations" columns keep each notes file's own entry, prefixed with the notes file number and its local source ID, so that every citation can be traced back to the note that recorded it. Where a row lists more than one URL, the Source ID links to the first. Every source was accessed on 12 October 2026. Booking-screen URLs are records of interface observations and are session dependent, so they will not necessarily reproduce the same screen.

## Closing lists

The four lists below restate the position of the evidence. "Verified" means supported by an opened official source or a direct observation on 12 October 2026, up to the boundary reached. It never means verified on site or through a completed booking.

### List 1. What has been verified

1. RSG describes itself as a closed joint stock company owned by the Public Investment Fund, formerly The Red Sea Development Company, renamed on 25 October 2022 [R004] [R037].
2. RSG's own count on 1 October 2026 is 19 resorts open: 11 at The Red Sea, seven at AMAALA, and Thuwal Private Retreat [R083].
3. Eleven resorts are selectable in the visitredsea.com booking widget and six in the amaala.com widget. Raffles and Grand Hyatt have pages but are not selectable [R095] [R226] [R099].
4. visitredsea.com and amaala.com run their own on-domain booking flow and do not link to operator booking engines, with one exception: Jayasom on amaala.com links to jayasom.com/booking [R098] [R113].
5. The published booking terms on visitredsea.com name Regenerative Escapes Tourism Company (CR 1010916245) as the contracting party, limit use to personal and non-commercial purposes, and prohibit extracting pricing and availability, including by screen-scraping software [R168].
6. RSG-operated resorts (Shebara, Desert Rock, Turtle Bay Hotel) have a second booking route through the Red Sea Global Hospitality engine, with different hotel identifiers and rate names [R227] [R225].
7. On every route reached, the guest-details page opened without signing in. The fields on those pages are recorded in Section 10 [R223] [R230] [R239].
8. Eight operator engines were walked to their guest page: Marriott, IHG, Six Senses, Accor (Raffles), Ennismore (SLS), Four Seasons, SynXis (Rosewood) and Jayasom [R239] [R240] [R241] [R182] [R243] [R244] [R245] [R248].
9. For 12 to 15 January 2027 the destination-site engine returned no availability for Shebara, Desert Rock and Turtle Bay Hotel, while the brand-site engine returned rates or available dates for the same resorts [R220] [R225] [R228] [R234] [R235].
10. An account drawer with email and password exists on visitredsea.com and amaala.com, with registration starting from one email field. No social sign-in and no live loyalty scheme were found [R095] [R113].
11. An official Visit Red Sea app exists on both app stores, published by Red Sea Global [R152] [R153].
12. No public brand guidelines, developer documentation, API documentation or partner programme were found for RSG [R023] [R292].
13. RSI airport publishes that transfers must be pre-booked at least 48 hours ahead and seaplanes at least seven days ahead [R260].
14. Official RSG sources contain no announcement of a guest-facing AI assistant or chatbot in the material checked [R023] [R138].

### List 2. What has only been partially investigated

1. The destination-site booking flow: steps 0 to 4 for one resort (Shebara). Step 05, other resorts beyond step 1, and the amaala.com flow beyond the first results screen were not walked [R223] [R149].
2. The RSG Hospitality engine: guest details read for Desert Rock and Shebara, rates only for Turtle Bay Hotel, nothing for Daraah Resort [R230] [R235].
3. Operator engines: rate list only for Nujuma, EDITION, Six Senses AMAALA, Four Seasons AMAALA, Equinox and Nammos. Children scenarios on a few engines only [R239] [R241] [R246].
4. Account creation: first screen only. Verification, password rules and the logged-in area were not seen [R095].
5. Cookie consent: configuration read for one location, banner not seen rendered [R095].
6. Accessibility and mobile behaviour: spot checks in desktop emulation, no audit and no real device [R095].
7. Operator legal terms: read by keyword or summary. Hyatt unreadable. Four Seasons and Rosewood site terms not located [R295] [R298].
8. Arabic: page-level checks only. Arabic forms, Arabic booking steps beyond step 1 and Arabic legal texts were not compared in detail [R236] [R104].
9. The company's press archive: about 50 of 407 items opened [R023].
10. Sources read through a summarising fetch tool (listed in Section 2.5) need a raw re-read before their wording is relied on.

### List 3. What remains unknown or conflicting

1. How a reservation is paid, verified, submitted and confirmed on any channel. Nothing after the guest-details page was observed.
2. Which identity and travel-document fields are mandatory. UI text strings name fields that the observed form did not show [R258] [R223].
3. Which RSG-run channel is authoritative when they disagree on availability, tax presentation and cancellation terms [R219] [R230] [R122].
4. Whether any supported access method, sandbox or partner programme exists, and whether RSG would permit an agent to use its booking channel [R292] [R168].
5. The vendors and systems behind the booking engines, chat and payment. All vendor names are indicative [R122].
6. Whether the live chat is staffed or automated, and its hours and languages. Published hours conflict [R110] [R105].
7. Everything about the Hyatt channel and the mobile app [R242] [R152].
8. The status of several properties: the seventh open AMAALA resort, Daraah Resort's operating status, opening dates for six Shura resorts, Clinique La Prairie and The Ritz-Carlton AMAALA, and the unnamed 18th hotel [R116] [R170] [R013].
9. The primary airport and transfer arrangements for AMAALA, and whether seaplanes serve it today [R199] [R214] [R130].
10. Data roles and flows for booking data among RSG, RETC, RSG Hospitality and operators [R291] [R168].
11. Whether an "Agentic Tourism Corridor" involving RSG exists as one vendor blog describes [R303].
12. The 128 conflicts in Section 15, none of which is resolved here.

### List 4. What we or RSG must provide or do next

1. Our team: decide the intended scope (which channel, which resorts, which languages) before approaching RSG, using the P1 questions in Section 18.
2. Our team: do not replay search requests or run any automated access again until written permission exists. Have counsel re-read the booking terms and site terms at source.
3. Our team: have a person repeat the key screens in a normal browser and save screenshots with URL and time, open the Hyatt pages manually, and re-read the sources that were summarised.
4. RSG: state which entity owns the booking channel and can authorise work with it, and whether any assistant, agent programme or programmatic access already exists.
5. RSG and RETC: give written permission, or decline it, for use of booking data, site content, logos and fonts.
6. RSG: provide an authorised test route (sandbox or agreed test booking) with a test identity, so that verification, payment, submission and confirmation can be observed.
7. RSG: supply the field specification, an itemised resort status list, the authoritative policy per channel, sample confirmation messages, and brand, naming and tone guidance in English and Arabic.
8. RSG contact centre: confirm chat staffing, hours, languages and the handoff route.
9. Operators: state their position on assisted or automated bookings on their own sites, if operator channels are to be in scope.

### Continuation checklist

- [ ] Agree scope internally (channel, resorts, languages) and assign an owner for RSG contact.
- [ ] Send the P1 questions in Section 18 and the technical discovery questions in Section 12.8 to RSG.
- [ ] Request written permission from RSG and RETC before any further automated access.
- [ ] Legal re-read of the Booking General Terms, site terms and privacy notices at source, in English and Arabic.
- [ ] Manual screenshot pass: destination-site steps 0 to 4, RSG Hospitality guest details, each operator guest page, account drawer, cookie banner in a clean profile.
- [ ] Manual read of Hyatt pages for Miraval and Grand Hyatt.
- [ ] Raw re-read of every source marked "summarised" in Section 2.5.
- [ ] Authorised walk of step 05, verification, payment and confirmation on the destination-site channel, once RSG provides a test route.
- [ ] Authorised test of account registration and "My Trips".
- [ ] Install and test the Visit Red Sea app with approved test data.
- [ ] One UI walk on amaala.com, and repeat walks for Desert Rock, Turtle Bay Hotel and one third-party resort on visitredsea.com.
- [ ] One overlapping date search on both RSG-run channels for a like-for-like price and policy comparison, with permission.
- [ ] Obtain from RSG: itemised resort status list, field specification, policy source of truth, sample confirmations, brand guidelines, approved names and tone guidance.
- [ ] Confirm chat staffing, hours and languages with the RSG contact centre.
- [ ] Re-check every time-sensitive item (flights, offers, opening status, visa rules) on the day it is used. All evidence here is dated 12 October 2026.
- [ ] Update the conflict register as RSG settles each item.
